## 15. Run-Record Vocabulary and Evidence Follow-Ups

The §10 plan review accepted three minors the enriched records leave open: latency carries a unit (`<int>s`) but no measurement boundary, unresolved-field coverage is reported for cost only, and transition evidence is quoted without a commit binding the quote to the record it came from. All three are small, all three lack an owner, and none blocks §8, which decides from regenerated exports with cost in tokens. The §14 plan review adds one more: gate evidence counts bound to their producing commit.

- [x] Define the latency boundary: what interval a round's `<int>s` measures, stated in the records header beside the unit. Done when: the header names the boundary, quoted, and mixed-boundary values have nowhere to hide. Done: the header names "latency in wall-clock seconds from the round's reviewer invocation to its returned output as timed by the recording session (`unresolved` when untimed; one boundary for every round, so mixed-boundary values have nowhere to hide)".
- [x] Report unresolved-field coverage per field and model in `--report`, extending the existing cost line. Done when: version, latency, and cost each show recorded-vs-unresolved counts, quoted from a driven report. Done: the report prints `cost: 6/65 recorded (gpt-5.6-sol 6/25, gpt-6-astra 0/21, opus 0/19)`, `latency: 0/65 recorded (...)`, `version: 46/65 recorded (gpt-5.6-sol 25/25, gpt-6-astra 21/21, opus 0/19)` (candidate tree; the stamp tree reads 8/68, 3/68, 48/68); pinned by `coverage-fields`.
- [x] Bind each transition's evidence to the commit whose tree holds the quoted record. Done when: every block carries the binding, the checker asserts it resolves, and one rebinding failure is quoted. Done: all 7 blocks carry `as-of:` (F3 0a24c03, T03-F3 f75d6f1, D07-F2 235a71f, S11-F4/F5 3ee9e5f, S12-F14/F16 ef362d6), each quote verified present at its bound commit; the checker asserts resolution; rebinding failure quoted: `D00-T04-S6-F3 binds as-of deadbee, which resolves to no commit`.
- [x] Pin the panel shape against voided rounds: the terminology block states Sol rounds 1-2 with the Opus sign-off at round 3 unconditionally, while the S9 block numbers a voided Sol round 2 and signs off at round 4. Done when: the header admits voids consuming numbers, quoted, and the S9 block reads consistent with it. Done: the header admits "Voided rounds keep their numbers: an error round consumes its number and the sign-off floats past it, so panel sections number the usable rounds 1..k in run order"; the S9 block (voided round 2, sign-off at round 4, "Panel sections number the five usable rounds") reads consistent with it.
- [x] Carry per-finding dispositions in the export, so a snapshot consumer can compute accepted yield without rejoining live records that may postdate the as-of. Done when: the export asserts dispositions per ref, quoted, and the live round-trip passes. Done: export v2 carries `dispositions` per round line (S12 round 5: F1-F3 all `fixed`); the checker asserts key-set equality with findings and ledger-set values (missing/valued/homed/required pins); the live round-trip passes (`export version 2, 30 runs, as-of ..., internally sound`; the stamp tree reads 31 runs).
- [x] Settle the provider vocabulary: round lines record runner names (`codex`, `claude`) where provider-level queries want the serving provider. Done when: the skill carries the recording step, and a round line written by following it resolves both fields, quoted from a driven record. ## 16. Blinded-Run Checker Defects

The §8 blinded runs re-reviewed `f118e30` with the fixes hidden and found three refusal-shape defects that survive in the shipped checkers, all driven: a duplicate transition block for a dead ref reports "names no live finding" twice without ever reporting the duplicate, `--report --export` silently prints only the report, and `schema: 99` emits the true version error plus a false "no schema declaration". (A fourth find, the panel-shape terminology contradicting the S9 block's voided round 2, filed as a §15 item.) A fifth, the never-defect severity loophole (the parser accepts a refuted finding carrying critical against the stated minor rule), filed from this review's sign-off as the fourth fix item. Each is a five-line fix with a refusal test; none blocks the §8 decision.

- [x] Report duplicate transition blocks even when the ref is dead. Done when: two blocks for one dead ref draw the duplicate message, quoted, and the live transitions still pass. Done: the seen-tracking moved above the live check; two F99 blocks draw `D00-T04-S9-F99 already has a block at line 61` quoted from the drive; the live transitions still pass (`ledger current, 209 finding(s), transitions complete`); pinned.
- [x] Reject conflicting `--report --export` flags with a usage error instead of silently printing the report. Done when: the combination fails naming the conflict, quoted, and each flag alone still works. Done: the combination exits 2 with `usage: todo-runs.py [--check] [--report | --export] [runs-file]; --report and --export conflict, pass at most one` quoted (plan review: neither flag is required, so "exactly one" misdirected); `--report` alone prints (31 runs, 69 rounds) and `--export` alone round-trips (version 2, 31 runs, sound); pinned with stderr capture.
- [x] Emit only the version error for a wrong schema declaration, not a false "no schema declaration" beside it. Done when: `schema: 99` draws exactly one message, quoted. Done: a wrong-version declaration marks the schema seen; `schema: 99` on a minimal fixture draws exactly `s16-schema99-min.md:1: schema 99 is not 1; this parser reads 1 only` quoted (pinned by exact equality). The preamble strictness policy (first error turns later header prose strict) stands unchanged: a full header file draws its outside-block lines separately, which is standing behavior, not the reported double.
- [x] Enforce the never-defect severity rule in the parser: a refuted, withdrawn, or duplicate finding carrying anything but minor fails the gate by name. Severity rates surviving contribution, not alleged impact: a duplicate of a critical is minor because it contributes nothing new. Done when: `REFUTED (self) [critical]` is reported naming the rule, quoted, the SEVERITIES comment states the semantic, and the live ledger still passes. Done: the fixture draws `never-defect severity rule: F1 is refuted but carries critical; refuted, withdrawn, and duplicate findings are minor` quoted with zero findings recorded; the SEVERITIES comment states surviving-contribution with the enforcement pointer; no live never-defect carries non-minor and the live ledger still passes (209); pinned both ways, the bad way covering all six cells (refuted, withdrawn, duplicate by major, critical) and the good way all three minor accepts.
- [x] Record the §15 stamp round: the §15 review's stamp round postdates the stamp commit it reviews, so it records here with `provenance: reconstructed`, following the §8 precedent (§15 item 9). Done when: the S15 block carries the stamp round (outcome `stamp`, purpose `stamp-review`, candidate the §15 stamp commit `56a8bb6`, the HOLDS attempt's model/effort/cost/latency per the run file's §15 stamp record) quoted, the panel mapping still spans panel rounds only, and `--check` passes. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: leaving the STAMP HOLDS verdict to the review file alone, which is the gap §15 item 9 exists to close. Done: S15 round 4 recorded (`outcome: stamp ... candidate: 56a8bb6 ... cost: 132909tokens latency: 424s ... purpose: stamp-review provenance: reconstructed`) quoted; the mapping spans panel rounds 1-3 only; `--check` passes (31 runs, 69 rounds).

## 17. Report Without Walking the Corpus Twice

The §8 diversity runs found `report()` calls `TF.collect()` after `run_check()` already collected the same corpus through `cross_check`, doubling repository-wide file reads and parsing on every `--report`. **Corrected 2026-09-20:** it said "doubling" and "25 files"; `report()` now collects twice itself (refuted-by-model and source-split), so `--report` walks the corpus three times, and the corpus is 36 files. True and cheap to fix by threading the collected findings through; unnoticed because the corpus is small and the report runs in milliseconds, which is also why this is a single-item section rather than a performance project.

- [x] Thread the collected findings from `run_check` through `report` so `--report` walks the review corpus once. Done when: one `TF.collect()` serves the check and the report, quoted from the code path, the report output is byte-identical before and after, and `--check` and `--export` outputs and exit codes are pinned unchanged, quoted. Done: `main()` collects once (`collected = TF.collect()`) and threads it through `run_check(runs_path, collected)` and `report(runs, runs_path, collected)`; `report()`'s two internal collects collapsed into the one override, the `cross_check`/`export_runs` idiom. The report is byte-identical past the as-of line (`cmp` silent; the as-of lines differ only in clock, same commit `deb947a`; the clock differs on every run by design, including re-runs of the same binary, so byte-identity is measured past that line). `--check` byte-identical exit 0/0; `--export` identical past its timestamp exit 0/0. Pin `report-single-collection`: red before (`0 3 collects`, the triple walk), green after (68 cases, 0 failed). `--export` keeps its own collect (contract scope is check plus report; its output pinned unchanged). Error paths walk once extra (the collect precedes `run_check`; milliseconds, recorded here).
- [x] Commit: `"workspace: report without walking the corpus twice"`

**Test checkpoint:** The report output is byte-identical across the change, quoted; the self-test pins the single collection.

## 18. Second Two-Model Revisit, Independently Rated

D00 T04 §8 kept both models on a value margin (79 vs 31) computed from severities the implementing session assigned in bulk after every outcome was known, then used to decide. That self-attestation confound is the same one D00 T04 §7 removed for the source split by deriving it from run records; the keep stands on the blinded Jaccard 0.22 corroboration, but the next revisit must not repeat the methodology. This section re-decides after five more panel-reviewed sections with severities assigned independently of the decision. Reading note: where D00 T04 §8 item 1 says both blinded outputs returned PASS, PASS means well-formed four-lens output, not approval.

**Trigger: starts after five more panel-reviewed sections exist in the run records past the §8 window of five.** Counted by query, not by prose: `python scripts/todo-runs.py --report` prints the count on its revisit-trigger line. Until the trigger is met this section waits, however its dependencies read: a revisit decided on the same window is a second opinion from the same data.

Pass 1 (52s, 7583 tokens, 2026-09-20, exit 0, all 22 lines well-formed) ran with the key on disk; the round-2 panel refused output-evidence as establishment, so pass 2 re-ran confined (37s, 13612 tokens, exit 0, all 22 lines well-formed) with the key, the pass-1 output, the ratings, the s-files, the ledger, the run records, the export, the snapshot, and TODO-04 all absent from the tree. Pass 2 governs, adopted whole and never merged: 21 of 22 severities agree with pass 1, sole difference B17 major to critical. The prompt withholds rung, section, fate detail, current severity, and the decision (B-numbers with category, fate word, and mechanism only, verified on its face); scale and disposition rules in-prompt. The adopted ratings deviate from the headings 19 of 22 upward and 0 downward, which a heading-anchored rater would not do. Rater per finding: blinded-sol-20260920-confined for all 22 (pass-1 rater blinded-sol-20260920); the adopted pass's ratings accepted verbatim (overriding any would re-attest; uniform strictness cannot bias the rung comparison the rater cannot see). S13: F1 critical (was major), F2 major (was minor), F3 major (was minor), F4 critical (was major), F5 major (was minor), F6 critical (was major), F7 major (was minor), F8 critical (was major), F9 major (was minor), F10 critical (was major), F11 major (was minor). S16: F1 major (was minor), F2 major (agrees), F3 minor (agrees, rule). S17: F1 major (was minor), F2 minor (agrees, rule). 19 of 22 disagree upward, 0 downward: the headings systematically under-rate, especially record falsehoods treated as polish against a scale whose major reads "wrong behavior in code, plan, or record". Worked ties: Jaccard exactly 0.5 at any values → KEEP; equal value-per-cost at Jaccard 0.7 → KEEP; Jaccard 0.6 with the trailer at 0.6x the leader → KEEP; Jaccard 0.6 with the trailer at 0.4x → CUT the trailer. MINIMUM ACTIVATION: the overlap leg cuts only when the window holds at least 2 blinded comparisons spanning at least 2 candidate classes of {review tooling, plan record, shipped code}; below that it is DORMANT (reported with its Jaccard, cutting nothing). ## 19. No Partial Flips

> **Started:** 2026-09-20T07:58:37Z

A `[x]` row with an open checklist reads as done while work remains, which is the one state the completion-first policy (`todo/README.md`) refuses to represent. **Corrected 2026-09-20:** this paragraph said nothing checks it; `orphaned-items-shipped` already compares row against items, but it excuses every struck item without requiring a `Deferred` marker or owner, trusts `Commit:` without defining its representation, and never states its fence/stamp scoping. This section supersedes it with the precise rule as a FATAL: a shipped row whose section still carries an unchecked micro-step outside the exemptions fails `validate` by file, section, and item, and `orphaned-items-shipped` retires (map entry, README row, and self-test cases) in the same commit, verdict-neutral on the live tree.

**Design, measured 2026-09-19 against the live tree:** four shapes are excused, each for a stated reason. `Commit:` lines are excused because the commit is proven by history rather than by the checkbox, and ticking one on a shipped section would rewrite a stamped checklist, which the spec forbids (5 live cases: `D00 T02 §4`, `D00 T04 §7`, `D00 T04 §9`, `D00 T04 §10`, `D07 T01 §1`). Struck `~~` items carrying a `Deferred` marker are excused because they are filed debt with an owner, not open work. Fenced code blocks are stripped before the scan (precedent: the panel-verdict scan), and the stamp region below `Verified:` is out of scope. With those exemptions the live tree reports zero, quoted, so the rule lands green with no migration. Re-verified 2026-09-20: the 5 `Commit:` cases still exact, 1 struck deferral (T02 §4), 0 plain opens. **Corrected 2026-09-20:** the re-verification's "6 fenced examples (T03 §4)" was a mislabel: those six are file-level `## Verification` items outside every section span (the parser clears `current` at any `## ` heading), never collected under any row. Zero `- [ ]` lines sit inside actual fenced blocks under shipped rows, and zero sit below a `Verified:` stamp, so the fence and stamp-region exemptions are forward guards pinned by self-test, not live cases. The bare-XREF shape the old rule excused is deliberately NOT excused: the old comment blessed a "bare `-> XREF:` line" but the code excused checkbox-wrapped `-> XREF:` items, and a cross-reference needs no checkbox, so an unticked one fails as the malformed item it is (zero live, verdict-neutral). The rule scans raw section spans in the validator, reusing `strip_fenced_code`, `STAMP_RE`, `DEFER_REF_RE`, and `resolve_ref`, rather than `s.items`: the exemptions need line context the parser does not keep (fence state, stamp position, continuation-line owners), and teaching the parser would ripple into the cache schema and duplicate the fence machine the stripper's docstring forbids duplicating. The §13 plan review adds two legs: the `Commit:` representation (§9 unticked-shipped versus §13 ticked-stamping) and deferral-owner validation.

**Updated 2026-09-20 by D00 T04 §20.** The stamp-region exemption above is removed: below-stamp checklist items fail as appended work, and the live rule excuses three shapes, not four. The paragraph above is the stamp-time design (historical); the canonical live statement is the rule comment beside `classify_unchecked` in `scripts/todo-validate.py`. Nothing else in this section changes: its checklist, checkpoint, and stamp stand as shipped, with the checkpoint's `four` reading stamp-time.

-> XREF: D00 T04 §13 -- the sibling flip-integrity machinery; this rule guards the row, that section binds the stamp

## 21. Review-Input Integrity Hardening

Started: 2026-09-20T13:30:00Z.

Two §12 sign-off findings ask for stronger machinery than §12's contract: the cross-check verifies file sets but not content coverage, and the stamp rung demands a receipt nothing verifies. Both are new tooling, neither blocks §12 (the designed contiguous flow is construction-safe and the stamp receipt is eyeballed per the skill), and both need an owner before the next review leans on the gap. A §13 hard-cap finding adds a sixth leg in the same hardening: the stamp patch flows through an unflagged `git diff --cached`. The §13 plan review adds seven more: explicit push with remote readback, merge-parent handling, runner-emitted attestation fields, mechanical anchor rejection, citation forms beyond skills, merge-range legality, and length-safe file lists. A §19 plan review adds two more: round-role candidate citation and typed manifest identity fields. A §20 plan review adds one more: hostile fence paths. (Depends On §12 carries the relation; no XREF lines, so this filing touches no stamped-or-stamping section.)

## 22. Checker Diagnostic Codes and Structured Errors

Started: 2026-09-20T17:59:00Z.

The §16 plan review found the checkers speak human text only: every refusal is a line-numbered English message with no stable code, so IDE, CI, and dashboard consumers must string-match prose that edits like §16's own "exactly one" to "at most one" can move. It also found CLI exit codes pinned for just one refusal family (the runs flag conflict, exit 2) while parse-level refusals surface through gate exits nothing drives. Both are new surface on the §16 refusal families, neither blocks §16. (Depends On §16 carries the relation; no XREF lines, so this filing touches no stamped-or-stamping section.)

Done: the registry lives once in `scripts/todo-diag.py` (`CODES` plus emit/refusal/dumps; the module docstring documents the family rule: one code per checking phase, so reworded messages never renumber). Drives (in-process `main()` against a fixture corpus; findings `main()` takes no paths): `[FIND-001] unrecognized arguments: --bogus` exit 2; `:3: [FIND-002] no severity marker…` exit 1; `[FIND-003] ledger is stale…` exit 1; `[FIND-004] D00-T99-S1-F1 reads withdrawn but keeps no transition` exit 1; `[FIND-005] refusing to write a ledger missing 1 finding(s)…` exit 1; `[RUN-001] usage…conflict…` and `[RUN-001] usage: …--check-export <export.json>` exit 2; `[RUN-002] no schema declaration…` exit 1; `:6: [RUN-003] effort must be one of…` exit 1; `[RUN-004] …but no run lists it` exit 1; `:2: [RUN-005] runner is panel but the review file has no panel sections` exit 1; `[RUN-006] candidate 0000000 is not a commit in this repository` exit 1; `[RUN-007] cannot read export…` and `[RUN-007] export_version must be an int…` exit 1. Unlisted: `DIAG.emit("FIND-999",…)` raises `ValueError: unlisted diagnostic code 'FIND-999'` quoted from the self-test leg. Round-1 fix (C1): both checker docstrings restated the mapping, so they slimmed to registry pointers (the self-test expected literals stay: executable pins that fail loud, not documentation). `check_transitions` now returns `(lineno, message)` so FIND-004 carries real locations (13 leg assertions updated). Round-3 fix (C1, C2): `describe`'s docstring said KeyError while the raise (and both suites' pins) is ValueError, fixed to ValueError; `refusal` now builds keys from SCHEMA_KEYS (a drifted key fails here or in the literal schema legs), where the constant previously sat unread.

## 23. Single-Reviewer Panel Revisit

**Re-scoped 2026-09-23** (operator instruction, answering the D00 T04 §27 plan review's PR10): this section was the third Sol-versus-Opus keep-or-cut revisit. The one-writer decision answered that question, since Opus 5.5 writes every section and so never reviews one (D00 T04 §27), and the fallbacks moved to the newest Grok model (D00 T04 §29). The question that still needs evidence is whether one GPT reviewer is enough, or whether Grok should take a live rung rather than wait for an outage. The §18 machinery answers it unchanged in shape: blinded severity, a controlled crossover, the zero and overlap legs, value per cost. The five §18-shape items are retired and their substance carries into the items below; the §18 plan-review findings they held (independent rating, pinned controls, deterministic candidate selection, blinded matching) ride items 3 and 4. Partial work from the old scope is parked unmerged on branch `wip/d00-t04-s23-revisit` (commit `189a3d01`), and item 1 disposes of it.

**Corrected 2026-09-25** (operator decision, commit `0c8ffa5a`): every panel slot now runs `gpt-6-astra` and Grok left the panel with every fallback slot, so the live-rung question is gone. The Sol-versus-Grok crossover with its calibration round, and the Grok-rung decision, are retired. The question left is whether one GPT reviewer with no fallback is enough: the rating item runs one rater family, and the decision item decides from the export whether a second non-writer family should return.

**Trigger: five panel-reviewed sections stamped under the GPT-governed wiring (on or after 2026-09-23, revisit sections excluded; D00 T04 §27 and §29 are the first two), or immediately if the GPT reviewer posts three consecutive full-scope zero sections or a GPT outage stops a round (**Corrected 2026-09-25:** a Grok fallback round can no longer govern).** Until then this section waits, however its dependencies read.

- [ ] Dispose of the parked branch without losing its evidence: the branch holds a finished Sol-versus-Opus crossover on shipped code (`listview.cpp` plus `listview.h` frozen at `dd2a2b6d`, 5 matched pairs and 18 solo finds, Jaccard 0.22) that no section has triaged, beside window code and a re-rating that the re-scope makes obsolete. Done when: the crossover artifacts and its `comparisons.md` line land on master as a closed-era record, quoted; each of the 23 finds is re-checked against the live source and every surviving defect is filed through `add-todo` with an owner and a `-> SOURCE:` naming the crossover, quoted with the count kept, refuted, and filed; the S19-S24 window code and the closed-window re-rating are dropped with that reason recorded; and the branch is deleted with its tip hash quoted. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: deleting the branch, which throws away 23 unreviewed defect reports.
- [ ] Roll the trigger to the GPT-governed era: `todo-runs.py --report` still counts toward the retired Sol-versus-Opus revisit. Done when: the trigger line counts panel sections stamped on or after 2026-09-23 (revisit sections excluded) toward this revisit, quoted, with the §18-era line reading retired beside it; the zero leg reports the GPT reviewer's trailing full-scope zeros as a strengthen signal (three consecutive fire the revisit early) rather than a cut signal; a recorded GPT outage that stopped a round fires it immediately, quoted from a fixture; and every counted section's disposition (value, carry, or drop with reason) reads in the report. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: leaving the old trigger, which reports a decided revisit as pending.
- [ ] Rate the window's severity independently, in the §18 item-1 shape: a blinded pass with the rater recorded per finding and ratings accepted verbatim. Done when: no severity in the window is decider-attested, quoted from the records; the de-anonymization key is written only after the rating output returns; and the rater runs through a panel slot (GPT, the one non-writer family since 2026-09-25), recorded per finding. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: the reviewing round's own severities.
- [ ] Decide whether one GPT reviewer with no fallback stays, or a second non-writer family returns, and name the next revisit. Done when: the decision is dated, cites a fresh export, replays the legs (zero with its consecutiveness rule, activation, minimum defect mass), normalizes value per cost by opportunity (full-scope and fix-loop inputs separated, or the pooling justified), and re-renders the dashboard with unique critical and major finds, median latency, normalized cost, outage history, trigger history, and confidence trend; a second family files its `.conclave/panel.toml` change as its own reviewed section rather than editing the table here; and the next revisit is named with its trigger. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: a decision without the export, which is an opinion.
- [ ] Commit: `"workspace: single-reviewer panel revisit"`

**Test checkpoint:** The parked crossover lands with its 23 finds triaged and filed, quoted; the GPT-era trigger reads met by query, and an outage fixture fires it; the window's severities are independent; the decision cites the export and names its successor. Blocked on the trigger until it is met, explicitly.

Self-review fix 5 (adversarial): the verifier bounds the member total (`BUNDLE_MAX_TOTAL`) and count (`BUNDLE_MAX_COUNT`) beside the per-member bounds, since per-member caps alone admit a thousand small members expanding past memory (`bundle-cap-total`, `bundle-cap-count`). Self-review fix 6 (adversarial): a carried transcript must be exactly one PASS-led line (`_bundle_transcript_ok`), so a failures-plus-injected-PASS record no longer verifies as passing; the emit and verify legs pin the injected shape at both ends and the old containing-PASS leg is retargeted. Round-4 fix (F15): single-PASS-led admitted the checker's own blocking output (`PASS N naming(s) to answer` exits 0 by the branch-on-reason design), so transcripts must now match one checker's holding shape (panel exact, plan alternation, stamp holding-only; disjoint, no role plumbing): blocking and forged PASS lines refuse at emit and fail at verify, the parameterized plan shape accepts, and the item-20 drive re-passes green. Round-5 fix (F21): the plan and stamp holding patterns are witnessed (real checker stdout piped into the transcripts), so a reason drift fails the legs instead of every suite staying green; the hand-written plan leg stays for parameterized-count generality.

## 25. Checker Diagnostic Follow-Ups

The §22 plan review files nine findings the §22 contract does not own: the registry covers two checkers only (PR1), code stability is unenforced (PR2), no retirement lifecycle exists (PR3), the refusal schema is unversioned (PR4), multi-refusal order is unpinned (PR6), two deliberate behaviors lack legs (PR10), no worked consumer exists (PR12, premium win), no operator index exists (PR13, premium win), and crashes collide with problem reports (PR14). All nine extend §22's registry, schema, or exit map rather than revisiting its decisions. (Depends On §22 carries the relation; opened beside §24 at filing so neither section breaches the sizing cap, per PR15.) The §24 plan review adds two more to the same surface: format-governed usage refusals (PR21) and one JSON document for report mode (PR22). The D00 T04 §24 stamp adds a twelfth: plan-ledger `duplicate` rows cannot name a panel finding as canonical (PR11 reword).

- [ ] Extend the diagnostic registry beyond the two review checkers: `todo-graph.py`, `todo-claims.py`, and `review_prompt.py` emit refusals with no codes, so consumers still string-match prose for most of the tooling surface (plan review of the §22 candidate, PR1; needs §22 shipped: it extends §22's registry). Done when: each tool's refusal families carry registry codes quoted from drives, the code list stays in the single registry (no second list), the self-tests pin each new family's code plus exit, and `validate` stays green. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: codes on one more tool, which repeats the two-checker partial coverage.
- [ ] Freeze the code-to-phase mapping the registry promises: §22 documents "one code per checking phase" as a docstring rule nothing enforces, so a future edit can silently renumber a code every downstream matcher keys on (plan review of the §22 candidate, PR2; needs §22 shipped: it constrains §22's registry). Done when: a frozen manifest (code to phase) ships beside the registry, the self-test fails quoted on a renumbered or reused code, and the README states the append-only rule. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: the docstring sentence alone, which is the current unenforced state.
- [ ] Define diagnostic-code retirement: the registry fails closed on unlisted codes but says nothing when a family goes away, so removing a check silently deletes a public contract (plan review of the §22 candidate, PR3; needs §22 shipped: it constrains §22's registry). Done when: the README carries the lifecycle beside the deferral contract (codes append-only; retired codes stay listed as retired with their removal version), a removed-without-retirement drive fails quoted, and `validate` stays green. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: deleting the code, which destroys the contract trail.
- [ ] Version the JSON refusal schema: the array carries code/path/line/message with no version key while the runs export carries `export_version`, so a consumer cannot detect a refusal-schema change (plan review of the §22 candidate, PR4; needs §22 shipped: it extends §22's schema). Done when: the schema carries a version the self-test pins quoted, a version-bump drive (a fixture with a newer version) fails loudly quoted, and the docstring states the versioning rule. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: unversioned keys, which §22's own `export_version` precedent treats as a defect.
- [ ] Pin multi-refusal emission order, stability, and dedup: gate modes can emit several refusals in one array with no contract on the sequence, so a consumer sorting or diffing runs keys on accident (plan review of the §22 candidate, PR6; needs §22 shipped: it constrains §22's emission). Done when: the emission order is documented (source order or sorted, the implementation chooses with the reason stated), a multi-refusal drive quotes the stable sequence across reruns, duplicates collapse or are preserved by stated rule quoted, and the self-test pins order plus rerun-stability legs. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: single-refusal legs only, which is the current coverage.
- [ ] Pin §22's two deliberate unpinned behaviors as legs: `--self-test` with a malformed `--format` reports the self-test usage, and a doubled `--format` resolves last-wins, both documented in the §22 Done but pinned nowhere, and unpinned-but-deliberate is what drifts (plan review of the §22 candidate, PR10; needs §22 shipped: it pins §22's edge semantics). Done when: both shapes carry self-test legs quoted, the §22 Done's deliberate-deltas sentence cites the legs, and the double-dash suite re-passes. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: the Done sentence alone, which asserts without enforcing.

## 26. Review-Flow Follow-Ups

The §24 plan review files nine findings plus one panel advisory the §24 contract does not own, and the D00 T04 §27 panel adds an eleventh (a POSIX producer-tree timeout): span exclusions lack an independent list (PR1), trusted pins are unenforced on approvals (PR4), recorded rounds lack candidate OIDs (PR5), multiple attestations mask by first-wins (PR7), undated stamps escape binding (PR8), the bundle name overclaims signing (PR10), the second-machine proof shares its host (PR12), the bundle carries no chain (PR13, premium win), and the fix-commit exception reads nowhere (PR16); the round-4 advisory adds the mega-assembly path (F17). All ten extend §24's review machinery rather than revisiting its decisions. (Depends On §24 carries the relation.)

- [ ] Review span exclusions against an independent list: the union leg proves declared inside pair but cannot tell a forgotten content commit from a legitimate record-commit exclusion, so an omitted commit ships unreviewed under a reviewed stamp (plan review of the §24 candidate, PR1; needs §24 shipped: it constrains §24's assembly binding). Done when: every in-span commit is either declared or exclusion-ledgered with a reviewed reason quoted, an undeclared-neither commit fails quoted, transcript-set completeness rides the same omission rule (a bundle missing a checker transcript fails), and the self-test pins omission plus ledgered-exclusion legs. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: the declared-only union, which is the current gap.
- [ ] Forbid trusted model pins on approval artifacts: `trusted` marks honestly-unmeasured runs, but nothing stops a sign-off attestation from carrying one, turning a label into authorization (plan review of the §24 candidate, PR4; needs §24 shipped: it constrains §24's provenance marking). Done when: sign-off rounds require derived provenance quoted (attest refuses a trusted pin where derivation was owed, or the read-back fails it -- the implementation chooses the enforcement point with the skill updated), the banner-less non-sign-off path still attests marked `trusted` quoted, and the self-test pins refused plus still-honest legs. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: the marker alone, which labels without enforcing.
- [ ] Bind each recorded round to its candidate OID: role correspondence proves the tag's round exists and is unique but not that the tagged OID is the round's recorded candidate (plan review of the §24 candidate, PR5; needs §24 shipped: it extends §24's correspondence). Done when: the round record declares `round N candidate <oid>` in a parseable shape quoted, a tag whose OID disagrees with the recorded candidate fails quoted, the live D00 T04 §21 and §22 records re-pass, and the self-test pins match plus mismatch legs. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: number-only correspondence, which is the current gap. (PR6's range-plus-list direction folds here: the ledger's non-tip candidates are the recorded superseded set.)
- [ ] Require exactly one authoritative attestation per stamp: `first wins` lets a stale first `Attestation:` reference mask a conflicting later one (plan review of the §24 candidate, PR7; needs §24 shipped: it constrains §24's membership input). Done when: two distinct attestation references on a stamp fail quoted naming both, the single-reference and legacy-absent shapes re-pass quoted, and the self-test pins conflict plus single legs. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: first-wins, which is the current masking shape.
- [ ] Close or prove shut the undated-stamp binding escape: stamps without a parseable Verified date skip hash and attestation binding, so new evidence could deliberately use the legacy shape (plan review of the §24 candidate, PR8; needs §24 shipped: it constrains §24's cutoff). Done when: either missing dates read as post-cutoff (failing unbound cites quoted) or the validator rule making undated stamps unshippable is cited quoted with a dateless-stamp drive failing at that rule -- the implementation chooses closes-or-proves with the reason stated -- and the self-test pins the chosen shape. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: the undated skip alone, which is the current escape.
- [ ] Resolve the signed-versus-unsigned naming tension in live prose: the bundle title says `signed` while the skill honestly states no key signs it, a tension reported three times (plan review of the §24 candidate, PR10; needs §24 shipped: it words §24's bundle). Done when: the skill plus docs name the artifact an unsigned integrity bundle (sealed titles untouched), the residual pointer (digest quote plus `--recheck-remote`) reads beside the name quoted, and the skill pins re-pass. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: renaming the sealed item title, which audit stance forbids. (The signing half duplicates round-1 F2: offline consistent-rewrite detection needs the external anchor the design provides.)

Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: a second same-host replay, which re-proves the same scope.

Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: the exhausted message alone, which names no path for that corner.
- [ ] Kill the whole producer tree on a POSIX slot timeout: `panel_slots.py exec` kills only the producer process off Windows (Windows already runs `taskkill /T`), so a child the producer spawned can keep writing the round output after exit 124 and overlap the fallback round (panel round 4 of the D00 T04 §27 review, F6; needs §27 shipped: it hardens §27's runner). Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: documenting the platform limit without a kill.

Done when: the skill states that a red read-back caused by the stamped change reopens the section through audit stance (and one caused elsewhere is fixed forward without reopening), a fixture drive shows the reopen shape validating, quoted.

Done when: the read-back requires the `push` event on `master` and reads the latest attempt, and fixture legs refuse a wrong-branch and a superseded-attempt run, quoted.

Done when: a `not triggered` verdict is confirmed by a grace-period `gh run list` that finds no run (a run that appears is waited on), unmodelled workflow keys make the calculation fail closed to waiting, and fixture legs pin both, quoted.

## 27. One Writer, a GPT-Governed Panel, Slot-Bound Pins

Started: 2026-09-23T10:54:29Z.

Operator decision 2026-09-23: Claude Code on Opus 5.5 is the only writer, Muse leaves the writer and researcher roles, and GPT-6 Sol is the reviewer. Today the panel's governing sign-off runs on Opus, the model that now writes every section, so the writer would approve its own work; and every reviewer pin is a literal typed into the skills (`gpt-5.6-sol` five times across two skills, `--model opus` on the sign-off), so each re-pin is a find-and-replace across prose that D00 T04 §19-style sweeps keep missing. This section ports ScratchPad's D00 T04 §15 slot table and §23 implementer-independent panel: one file binds every review role to a model, effort, and timeout; one module validates it and runs the producer; the skills name slots, never models; and the stamp validator requires a GPT-governed record for stamps from 2026-09-23. Justified defaults, each cheap to change: the same-family fallback is `gpt-5.6-terra` and the double-GPT-outage cross-fill is the writer's own family with a mandatory `GPT outage` note (ScratchPad's matrix, kept so a GPT outage degrades rather than halts); retired pins stay registered so historical run records still parse; the researcher pass moves to the writer session because it needs sources and the web, not independence.

**Corrected 2026-09-23:** D00 T04 §23 (third two-model revisit) is superseded in substance: its keep-or-cut question between Sol and Opus as panel rungs is answered by the operator decision above. Its partial work (items 1-3) is parked unmerged on branch `wip/d00-t04-s23-revisit` (commit `189a3d01`); §23 stays open until it is either re-scoped to the new matrix or closed with that reason.

## 28. Slot-Table Review Follow-Ups

The §27 plan review files five findings the §27 contract does not own: a `GPT outage` note alone authorizes the writer-family cross-fill (PR2; **Groomed 2026-09-23:** D00 T04 §29 replaced the cross-fill with the Grok fallback, which the item now binds), the governing heading is a word rather than a derived identity (PR3), a re-pin can change how an older round reads (PR4), `exec` extra arguments are not barred from overriding the pin (PR5), and derived provenance binds the sign-off only (PR6). All five harden §27's table, runner, or rule 16.

**Corrected 2026-09-25** (operator decision, commit `0c8ffa5a`): every panel slot now runs `gpt-6-astra`, Grok left the panel with every fallback slot, `panel_slots.py` no longer runs Grok, and rule 16 refuses any Grok-governed stamp dated 2026-09-25 or later. Five items hardened code or a matrix that no longer exists and are retired: the failed-attempt citation for a Grok fallback (§27 PR2, extended by §29 PR6), the Grok prompt-file cleanup (§29 F8), live-regex newest resolution (§29 PR3), per-route failover (§29 PR4), and the Grok read-only refusal (§29 PR5). The wording item (§29 F9) carries forward as the no-fallback wording sweep, and the provenance item drops its Grok clause.

- [ ] Bind the governing heading to the derived runner identity: rule 16 reads the heading word, so a mislabeled round could govern (plan review of the §27 candidate, PR3; needs §27 shipped: it extends §27's rule 16). Done when: post-cutover stamps require the attestation's derived reviewer family to match the last panel heading's family quoted, a mismatch fails quoted, and the live §27 record re-passes. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: a heading-spelling check.
- [ ] Persist the resolved pin and table revision with every round: a later re-pin changes what a slot name means, so an old round's slot reads ambiguously (plan review of the §27 candidate, PR4; needs §27 shipped: it extends §27's runner). Done when: `exec` prints the table's content hash beside the resolved model and effort, the round record carries both quoted, and a re-pinned fixture proves the old round still names its original model quoted. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: relying on git history of the table.
- [ ] Refuse extra arguments that override the pin: `exec <slot> [extra...]` appends caller arguments to the producer argv, so `-m`, `--model`, `--effort`, or a `model_reasoning_effort` override would bypass the table (plan review of the §27 candidate, PR5; needs §27 shipped: it constrains §27's runner). Done when: `exec` refuses any extra argument that names a model or effort flag (an allowlist per slot kind: `--commit <sha>` for `independent`, nothing for the rest) quoted, and the self-test pins the refusal plus the allowed shape. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: documenting the rule.
- [ ] Extend derived provenance to every governing role: D00 T04 §26 refuses trusted pins on sign-off approvals only, while `stamp-check` and `depth` also authorize (**Corrected 2026-09-25:** the Grok fallbacks this named left the panel) (plan review of the §27 candidate, PR6; needs §27 shipped: it names §27's slots). ## 29. Grok Fallbacks on the Newest Grok Model

Started: 2026-09-23T17:05:37Z.

Operator decision 2026-09-23, after D00 T04 §27 shipped: every fallback slot runs Grok on the newest Grok model the CLI offers (Grok 4.7 today; a Grok 4.8 release must be picked up with no edit). D00 T04 §27 left the fallbacks on `gpt-5.6-terra` (same family as Sol, so one provider outage takes both rungs) and a writer-family `cross-fill` (Claude reviewing Claude's work, the independence gap its plan review raised as PR1). Moving the fallbacks to a third family removes both: a GPT outage fails over to an independent reviewer, and the writer never reviews. Justified defaults, each cheap to change: the resolver reads `grok models` at run time and takes the highest `grok-X.Y` (variants with a suffix, such as `-build-fast`, are not candidates); a failed listing fails the round rather than guessing a model; the producer runs headless with a read-only tool allowlist, since Grok's OS sandbox profiles do not apply on Windows; `cross-fill` is removed, so when Sol and Grok are both down the review stops and waits for the operator.

- [x] Add the Grok family to `.conclave/panel.toml` and `scripts/panel_slots.py`: a `grok` family, a registry entry that resolves to the newest listed Grok model at run time, the four fallback slots (`bulk-fallback`, `signoff-fallback`, `plan-fallback`, `arch-fallback`) on it, plus the stamp review's route through `signoff-fallback`, `cross-fill` removed, and every slot refused when it shares the writer's family. Done when: `panel_slots.py show` prints Grok on every fallback with the resolved model named, a fixture listing with `grok-4.8` resolves to `grok-4.8` and one with only suffixed variants refuses, and the self-test pins both plus the no-writer-family rule quoted. Done: `panel_slots.py show` prints `grok-4.7 (newest of grok-newest)` on `bulk-fallback`, `signoff-fallback`, `plan-fallback`, and `arch-fallback` (the stamp review fails over through `signoff-fallback`), `gpt-5.6-terra` retired and `cross-fill` gone; `resolve signoff-fallback` prints `grok-4.7` live; the self-test (`48 cases, 0 failed`; 44 at the implementation commit; the new-release legs use a `k-` stand-in fixture rather than `grok-4.8`, and the live-regex proof files to D00 T04 §28 as PR3; the round-1 answer added the alias refusal, the prose-listing leg, and the extra-argument refusal, and the round-2 answer the no-leftover-prompt leg) pins a listing with `k-4.8` resolving to it, only list items resolving (prose around the listing never does), extra arguments refused on a Grok slot so nothing follows its tool restrictions, the `grok-newest` alias refused as a recorded model, `k-4.10` beating `k-4.7`, a suffix-only listing refusing `resolves to nothing`, the writer-family refusal on every slot, and a `served` name (Grok reports `grok-4.7-build` for a `grok-4.7` request, found by the live probe) that records accept but that never decides what runs.

## 30. CI Read-Back and Reachable Provenance

Started: 2026-09-23T22:01:35Z.

Split out of D00 T04 §26 on 2026-09-23 by operator instruction, ahead of the campaign. CI (`plan-gates`) failed on every push from 2026-09-20 to 2026-09-23, and five stamps landed on the red run unnoticed. The cause was provenance candidates in the D00 T04 §22 and §24 findings: staged trees captured with `git write-tree` that no pushed commit contains, so they resolved on the writing machine and every local gate passed, while CI's fresh clone failed 17 fatals. The immediate repair tagged each tree under `refs/tags/provenance/` (commit `657a1d88` turned CI green, run 35924459201). This section makes the failure impossible to repeat and makes a red CI stop the campaign that would otherwise build on it. Both items touch the review skill's stamp-and-push path, which is why they ship together.

- [x] Keep every provenance candidate reachable from a pushed ref: the D00 T04 §22 and §24 findings cite staged trees captured with `git write-tree` (`c6b1413d`, `d7e58618`, `49ff4978`) that no pushed commit contains, so they resolved locally and every local gate passed while CI's fresh clone failed 17 fatals on every push from 2026-09-20 to 2026-09-23 (found 2026-09-23 while grooming; repaired by tagging each tree under `refs/tags/provenance/`). Done when: the stamp flow tags any uncommitted tree or commit it cites as `provenance/<section>-<short>` and pushes the tag with the stamp, the validator (or the push fence) refuses a candidate no pushed ref reaches, quoted from a fixture, and the skill states the rule. Done: enforced in the push fence rather than the validator (**Corrected 2026-09-24:** the validator runs offline in pre-commit and in CI, where a fresh clone already fails an unreachable candidate; the gap was catching it on the writing machine before the push). `review_prompt.py` gains `provenance-tags` (tags each candidate the pushed commit does not reach as `provenance/<prefix>-<short8>`, wrapping a tree in a parentless commit; idempotent) and `check-reachable` (`--refs` before the push, `--remote` after it); the review skill's push block runs both and pushes the tags with the stamp. Fixture legs: a local-only staged tree is refused against the remote and before the push, tagging wraps it, the tag in the push set passes, the landed tag passes and a fresh clone resolves the tree, an unknown object is refused (7 legs, plus 3 skill pins); mutating the tree check fails 6 legs by name. Real drive: `check-reachable --remote origin` passes the §22, §24, §27, and §29 findings, and the §24 findings against `--refs HEAD` alone exit 1 naming `d7e58618` and `49ff4978`.
- [x] Read CI back after every push: the stamp flow's push reads back the ref but never the workflow, so five stamps landed on a red `plan-gates` run without anyone noticing (found 2026-09-23). Done when: after each push the flow waits for the `plan-gates` run for that commit and records its conclusion in the run file, a red conclusion stops the run instead of starting the next section, and the check is quoted from a drive. Done: `review_prompt.py ci-wait <sha>` resolves the full sha (`gh run list --commit` ignores a short one, probed 2026-09-24), waits for the `plan-gates` run, and exits 0 green, 1 red, 2 unverifiable; the review skill's push block runs it and the run file records its line, and `process-phase` stops the loop on a red or unverifiable read-back. Fixture legs against a faked `gh`: success, failure, pending past the deadline, no run, and the CLI resolving a short sha to a red exit (5 legs, plus 2 skill pins). Real drive: `ci-wait 06e570b5` prints `plan-gates failure .../runs/35923586765` and exits 1; `ci-wait 657a1d88` prints `plan-gates success .../runs/35924459201` and exits 0. **Corrected 2026-09-24 by the independent review's P1:** GitHub runs `plan-gates` only when the pushed range touches its path filter, and only for the pushed head, so a code-only push would have waited out the deadline and stopped the phase. `ci-wait` now reads the workflow's `paths:` filter and the range `--since <remote head before the push>`..head, and reports `not triggered` with exit 0 when nothing in the range matches (4 filter legs plus a not-triggered CLI leg); the skills capture the remote head before each push. Real drive: `ci-wait bac6bde9` (a docs-only push GitHub never ran, `gh run list` returns `[]`) prints `plan-gates not triggered (1 changed path(s), none in the workflow's path filter)` and exits 0. The filter is read from the pushed commit's own `.github/workflows/plan.yml` (`git show <head>:...`), never the working tree, so a local edit cannot turn a red run into `not triggered` (panel round 1 of the §30 review; 1 leg where the working-tree copy would trigger and the committed copy does not).
- [x] Commit: `"workspace: CI read-back and reachable provenance"`

**Test checkpoint:** A fixture candidate that only a local object store holds is refused before the push, quoted; the stamp flow tags and pushes a cited staged tree, and a fresh clone resolves it, quoted; the post-push read-back records the `plan-gates` conclusion for the pushed commit, and a red conclusion stops the run, quoted from a drive.

**Corrected 2026-09-25** (D00 T04 §33, answering the D00 T04 §31 plan review's PR8): the stop-on-red wording in the second item ("a red conclusion stops the run", "`process-phase` stops the loop on a red or unverifiable read-back") and in this checkpoint is superseded by D00 T04 §31: a red read-back is a failed gate the run repairs forward, and the run stops only for a cause the tree cannot fix. The stamp below is untouched; it records what shipped on 2026-09-24.

## 31. Red CI Repaired, Not Waited On

Started: 2026-09-23T23:20:26Z.

Operator direction 2026-09-24, correcting D00 T04 §30: a red CI read-back must be repaired by the run, not parked for the operator. §30 made the read-back stop the next section, and its wording ("stops the loop until the build is green again") would park a campaign on a red build, which `AGENTS.md` forbids: a runner finishes what it starts, waiting is never a strategy, and a gate that refuses is a cause the run fixes. This section turns the stop into a repair loop with a bound and a narrow escalation.

- [x] Show the failure, not just the verdict: on a red conclusion `ci-wait` fetches the failed steps' log (`gh run view <id> --log-failed`) and prints, bounded, the failing job and step names and the log's last lines, so the runner diagnoses from evidence rather than re-running blind. Done when: fixture legs print the step names and a bounded excerpt on red, a log fetch that fails still reports red with the reason, and a real drive on the D00 T04 §22 era red run (`06e570b5`) prints the `Validate the TODO tree` step and its `resolves to nothing` lines, quoted. Done: `ci-wait` appends `failed_log_report` to a red line: the failing `job / step` names from `gh run view <id> --log-failed` (lines read `job<TAB>step<TAB>log`, probed 2026-09-24), then up to 20 signal lines (fatal, error, fail, traceback) with timestamps, ANSI codes, and group markers stripped and repeats collapsed with a count, falling back to the last lines when nothing signals. Legs: the red fixture names its step and its FATAL line without the advisory noise, a failed log fetch still reads red with the reason, the excerpt is bounded with its fallback and keeps the last distinct lines (4 legs). Real drive: `ci-wait 06e570b5` exits 1 printing `failing step(s): plan-gates / Validate the TODO tree`, the three distinct `resolves to nothing` defects, and the validator's `17 fatal` summary.
- [x] Repair a red read-back as a failed gate: the runner diagnoses from the printed log, fixes the cause forward in a repair commit (or, when the red is the just-stamped section's own change, reopens that section through audit stance), pushes, and reads CI back again; on green it continues with the next section in the same turn. Done when: `process-phase`, `process-todo-section`, and the review skill's push block state the loop, the §30 "stops the loop" wording is gone, and skill pins hold the loop's sentences, quoted. Done: the review skill's push block, `process-phase` Step 3, and `process-todo-section` step 7 state the loop (diagnose from the printed evidence, fix forward in a repair commit or reopen the just-stamped section through audit stance, push through the same block, read back, continue on green in the same turn); `grep -rn "stops the run\|stops the loop" .claude/skills/` prints nothing; pins `skill-ci-red-repairs` and `skill-ci-continue` hold the sentences. **Corrected 2026-09-24 by the independent review (two P1s):** a reopened section is repaired, re-reviewed, and re-stamped before anything continues, and a red on a SHIP push makes the repair commit the new candidate for the checkpoint, the independent review, and the panel; pins `skill-ci-reopen-restamp` and `skill-ci-repaired-candidate` hold both.
- [x] Bound the repair: at most three repair attempts per red, and a unit patched in three consecutive attempts is rethought rather than patched again, matching the review loop's rule. Done when: the skills state the bound and the run file records each attempt (commit, CI line), quoted from the skill text. Done: the skills state at most three repair attempts per red, each recorded in the run file with its commit and `ci-wait` line, and the three-consecutive-patches rethink rule; pin `skill-ci-repair-bound` holds it.
- [x] Escalate only what the tree cannot fix: a red whose failing step never ran the repository's code (checkout, runner setup, a lost runner), an unverifiable read-back that stays unverifiable after one retry (GitHub unreachable, `gh` missing or unauthenticated, runner quota), or a repair bound exhausted. Done when: `ci-wait` names the failing step so the distinction is visible, the skills name exactly these escalation causes, the run file records the cause before the run reports to the operator, and a fixture leg shows an unverifiable read-back retried once, quoted. Done: the skills name exactly the three causes (a failing step that never ran the repository's code, a read-back still unverifiable after one retry, the repair bound exhausted) and require the run file to record the cause before the report; `ci-wait` retries an unverifiable read-back once (`--retry-wait`, default 60 s) and then prints `still unverifiable after one retry: escalate`. Legs: a flaky listing retried once reads green, a pending one escalates after the retry (2 legs); pin `skill-ci-escalation`.
- [x] Commit: `"workspace: red CI repaired, not waited on"`

**Test checkpoint:** The red fixture prints the failing step and a bounded excerpt, a failed log fetch still reads red, and the retry leg retries an unverifiable read-back once, all quoted from `review_prompt.py --self-test`; the real red run prints its failing step and lines; the skill pins hold the repair loop, the bound, the escalation list, and the continue-on-green sentence.

**Corrected 2026-09-25** (D00 T04 §35, answering the D00 T04 §33 plan review's PR1): two rules above are superseded by D00 T04 §33. The bound item's "at most three repair attempts per red" is now at most three repair attempts per repair episode, counted across consecutive reds so a new red never resets it. The escalation item's "a red whose failing step never ran the repository's code (checkout, runner setup, a lost runner)" is now judged by cause: a repository-controlled step (the workflow file, a pinned action, a setup script) is repairable even when it fails during setup, and only a runner or platform fault escalates. The stamp below is untouched; it records what shipped on 2026-09-24.

## 32. Campaign Guard: Stop Hook, Heartbeat, Breaker

Started: 2026-09-23T23:53:18Z.

Operator question 2026-09-24: where is the stop hook, the cron that reminds the run to carry on, and the run guard. Resolute has only a heartbeat described in `process-plan` prose, created when a campaign starts, whose liveness test still read Muse session logs until D00 T04 §27 rewired it, and no Stop hook, no `.claude/settings.json`, and no guard file. ScratchPad shipped the full guard on 2026-09-23 (its commit `0da6bd2`): a Stop hook that blocks the campaign session's end of turn while the run is open and names the next ready row, a stall breaker inside it, a `CronCreate` heartbeat that resumes the idle session with full context, and a gitignored guard file naming the one session the hook may block. This section ports it, adapted to this tree.

- [x] Port the Stop hook to `.claude/hooks/campaign-stop.ps1` and wire it in `.claude/settings.json`: it blocks only the session named in `build/claude-campaign-guard.json`, names the next ready row, and lets the turn end when the guard is gone, the run file carries `## Closeout` or a column-0 `PARKED` line, or `query ready` prints `0 runnable now`; it fails open on a bad payload or a thrown error. Done when: a driven self-test (`scripts/campaign_guard.py --self-test`, in `scripts/check-all.ps1`) feeds the hook fixture payloads and asserts allow for no guard, another session, closeout, park, and nothing runnable, and block naming the next row for an open run, quoted. Done: the hook is ported from ScratchPad with three adaptations: it calls `python` (Resolute's gates resolve that name), its block message says a red CI is repaired (D00 T04 §31), and it forces UTF-8 on the console and on Python's output and keeps the script ASCII, because the first self-test run found that Windows PowerShell 5.1 turned the section mark in a plan row into invalid bytes and broke the block payload's JSON (ScratchPad's copy carries the same latent defect). `.claude/settings.json` wires it as the Stop hook. `python scripts/campaign_guard.py --self-test` drives the real hook over a throwaway workspace: allow without a guard, for another session, when nothing is runnable, when parked, after closeout, when the run file escapes the workspace, and on a malformed guard (fail open); block naming the next row for an open run, and a mid-line `PARKED` still blocks (10 legs of 15 with Git Bash, 14 without it); it runs in `scripts/check-all.ps1` as `campaign guard self-test`. **Corrected 2026-09-24 by the independent review's P1:** the first wiring passed `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` in the hook command, which PowerShell (Claude Code's hook shell on a Windows host without Git Bash) does not expand, so the hook would never launch. The command now reads the variable through .NET with no `$` in it, and the self-test runs the exact configured command through PowerShell and Git Bash (3 legs: settings wired, both shells launch); restoring the old command fails the PowerShell leg.
- [x] Keep the stall breaker: after three blocks with no change to HEAD, the working-tree diff, the untracked set, or the run file, the hook lets the turn end and counts a trip in `build/claude-campaign-state.json`; real progress resets it. Done when: the self-test drives three unchanged blocks to a trip and a changed tree back to a fresh block count, quoted. Done: the self-test drives three unchanged blocks to a trip (`trips` 1, `stalled` true, `stall breaker tripped (1)` on stderr) and an untracked file back to a fresh block (`blocks` 1, `trips` 0) (2 legs); raising the breaker limit to 99 fails the trip leg, restored it passes 15 of 15 with Git Bash.
- [x] Rewrite `process-plan`'s run guard around the guard file and the heartbeat: start order (adopt or `CronCreate` the heartbeat, write the guard file with the session id and job id, record both in the run file), the canonical heartbeat prompt (finished, stalled twice, or resume), the exact end markers, the 7-day job expiry and new-session rewrite, and the operator stop that deletes the guard, the state file, and the job before confirming. Done when: the skill reads so, the old liveness-based prompt is gone, and `process-phase` points at it, quoted. Done: `process-plan`'s Run guard now states the three parts, the start order (adopt or `CronCreate` on `3-59/5 * * * *`, write `build/claude-campaign-guard.json` with the session and job ids, record both), the exact end markers, the 7-day expiry and new-session rewrite, the operator stop order, that a red CI is repaired rather than a stop, and the canonical `Claude run-guard heartbeat for Resolute` prompt; the old liveness-based prompt is gone (`grep 'Run-guard heartbeat for the Resolute\|25 minutes'` prints nothing), and `process-phase` verifies the guard file and job instead of listing jobs alone.

## 33. CI Repair-Loop Follow-Ups

Started: 2026-09-25T16:23:22Z.

The D00 T04 §31 plan review files eight findings the §31 contract does not own: the loop is specified and its diagnostics are proven, but no drive has run it end to end (PR1), a repair outside the stamped section has no owning section (PR2), a red whose log cannot be fetched has no next step (PR3), a slow run can read as unreachable (PR4), a repairable setup failure can escalate (PR5), the bound resets per red (PR6), a push that changes the workflow itself can read as not triggered (PR7), and the stamped D00 T04 §30 text still says a red stops the run (PR8).

- [x] Run the repair loop end to end: a deliberately red push is diagnosed from `ci-wait`'s evidence, repaired, pushed, and read back green, with the run-file record and, when the red is a stamped section's own, the re-stamp (plan review of the D00 T04 §31 candidate, PR1; needs §31 shipped: it proves §31's loop). Done when: one recorded episode on a scratch branch or a disposable workflow path quotes every step's line, and the run file carries the episode. Done: one episode on the disposable branch `drill/d00-t04-s33` with its own workflow (`plan-gates` runs on master only), recorded in docs/phase-runs/2026-09-25-phase-0.md. Red 1 (`784d4fe6`) started no job, a YAML error in the drill workflow; `ci-wait` fell back past the missing logs and, after the fix this drill forced, reads it `cause: repairable`. Repair attempt 1 of 3 (`6b8d79d2`) exposed red 2, the intended missing marker, diagnosed from the real log; repair attempt 2 of 3 (`567f64e6`) read back `repair-drill.yml success .../runs/36161821354`. The branch and worktree were deleted. The red was this section's own drill, so no stamped section was reopened and no re-stamp was owed. The drill also showed that `gh run list --workflow` resolves names only on the default branch, so a branch-only workflow is read back by file name.
- [x] Give every repair commit an owner: a red caused outside the just-stamped section is repaired under a section of its own (the reopened owner, or a new section filed through `add-todo` with a checkpoint and review), never as an unowned commit (plan review of the D00 T04 §31 candidate, PR2; needs §31 shipped: it constrains §31's repair). Done when: the skills name the owner rule, and the repair commit's message names its section, quoted. Done: both `review-todo-section` and `process-phase` now state that every repair commit has an owner (the reopened just-stamped section, the reopened owner of the broken code, or a new section filed through `add-todo`), and that the repair commit's message names it; pin `skill-ci-repair-owner` (+1). The drill's repair commits name D00 T04 §33, quoted in the run file.
- [x] Diagnose a red whose log cannot be fetched: when `gh run view --log-failed` fails, the runner falls back to the full log, then to re-running the failing step's command locally at the pushed commit (plan review of the D00 T04 §31 candidate, PR3; needs §31 shipped: it extends §31's evidence). Done when: `ci-wait` prints the workflow's command for the failing step when the log is unavailable, and a fixture leg quotes it. Done: `failed_log_report` falls back from `--log-failed` to the full log (filtered to the failing steps `gh run view --json jobs` names), then to the pushed commit's workflow: `workflow_step_commands` parses each `run:` step, and `ci-wait` prints `rerun locally at <sha>: <step>: <command>` for the failing step, or every run step when none is named. Legs (+4): `workflow-step-commands-parsed`, `ci-wait-no-log-prints-the-failing-step-command`, `ci-wait-no-step-evidence-lists-every-run-step`, `ci-wait-falls-back-to-the-full-log`. The live drill's red 1 took this path (both logs `not found`). **Corrected 2026-09-25 by the independent review (F2):** gh labels full-log segments it cannot map `UNKNOWN STEP`, so the step filter could drop every line and skip the command fallback; a filter that keeps nothing now keeps the whole log (`the whole log, its steps unmapped`), and a full log with no lines falls through to the commands. Leg `ci-wait-keeps-unmapped-full-log-evidence` (+1). **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 1:** a failed-step log that fetches but carries no lines now falls through like a missing one, and a block `run:` step prints as written, its relative indentation and newlines kept, rather than joined with `&&`. Leg `ci-wait-empty-failed-log-falls-through` (+1); the block leg now carries an indented `if` body. **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 2:** a `- run: |` block now ends at its own key's column, so a sibling `shell:` or `env:` is never read as script. Leg `workflow-step-commands-stop-at-a-sibling-key` (+1). **Limitation 2026-09-25** (the sign-off round's F8): a quoted inline `run:` prints with its quotes and a folded `>` block prints unfolded; D00 T04 §35 owns the decoder and, until it ships, the fail-closed refusal.
- [x] Tell a slow run from an unreachable one: a run still queued or in progress after the deadline keeps being waited on with a longer ceiling, and only a `gh` or GitHub failure counts as unverifiable (plan review of the D00 T04 §31 candidate, PR4; needs §31 shipped: it corrects §31's escalation). Done when: a pending-then-green fixture passes without escalation, and a `gh` error still escalates after its retry, quoted. Done: `ci_conclusion` bounds only the wait for a run to be listed by `--timeout`; once a run is listed and still queued or in progress it waits to `--ceiling` (default 3600s), and a `gh` failure still returns unverifiable at once. Legs: `ci-wait-waits-out-a-slow-run-without-escalating` (+1, pending twice then green, no retry line), `ci-wait-escalates-a-gh-error-after-one-retry` (replaces the pending-based escalation leg), and the retry leg's flaky fixture now lists no run first; pin `skill-ci-slow-run` (+1).
- [x] Classify a failing setup step by cause: a failure in a repository-controlled step (the workflow file, a pinned action, a setup script) is repairable, and only a runner or platform fault escalates (plan review of the D00 T04 §31 candidate, PR5; needs §31 shipped: it corrects §31's escalation list). Done when: the skills state the rule by cause, and a fixture log for a bad pinned action reads as repairable, quoted. Done: `classify_red` prints one `cause:` line on every red: a runner or platform fault (a shutdown signal, lost communication, a GitHub 5xx or outage) escalates, and anything the repository controls is repairable, including a pinned action that fails during job setup and a run that started no job because its workflow file did not load. Both skills state the rule by cause, pin `skill-ci-cause-rule` (+1). Legs (+4): `ci-wait-bad-pinned-action-reads-repairable` (`cause: repairable (repository-controlled: plan-gates / Set up job)`), `ci-wait-lost-runner-reads-platform`, `ci-wait-red-step-reads-repairable`, `ci-wait-no-job-reads-a-repairable-workflow-file` (the case the drill found). **Corrected 2026-09-25 by the independent review (F1):** the cause was judged on the bounded excerpt, which keeps only signal lines, so a shutdown signal followed by cancellation errors read repairable; `classify_red` now reads every normalized log line (`normalized_log_lines`). Leg `ci-wait-classifies-the-whole-log-not-the-excerpt` (+1).
- [x] Cap the whole repair episode: at most three repair attempts across consecutive reds on the same run, not per red, so a new red cannot reset the bound (plan review of the D00 T04 §31 candidate, PR6; needs §31 shipped: it tightens §31's bound). Done when: the skills state the episode cap and the run file counts attempts across the episode, quoted. Done: both skills bound the repair per episode, from the first red to the next green, at most three repair attempts per repair episode across its consecutive reds, so a new red never resets the count, and the run file numbers each attempt `attempt N of 3`; pins `skill-ci-repair-bound` (changed from the per-red wording) and `skill-ci-episode-no-reset` (+1). The drill's episode counts 2 of 3 across two reds, quoted in the run file.
- [x] Verify a push that changes the workflow: when the pushed range edits or removes `.github/workflows/plan.yml` or its path filter, `ci-wait` never reads `not triggered` from the new filter alone (plan review of the D00 T04 §31 candidate, PR7; needs §31 shipped: it hardens the D00 T04 §30 read-back §31 extends). Done when: a range touching the workflow waits for a run or escalates, and a fixture leg pins it, quoted. Done: `ci-wait` checks whether the pushed range edits `.github/workflows/`. If it does, it skips the not-triggered shortcut and waits for a run, escalating if none comes, with the workflow read from `--workflow-path` (default `.github/workflows/plan.yml`). Legs (+2): `ci-wait-waits-when-the-range-edits-the-workflow`, `ci-wait-escalates-when-an-edited-workflow-never-runs`; the committed-workflow leg now commits its workflow first so it still pins the committed-filter rule. The drill's red 1 read `the range edits the workflow ...; waiting for a run`. **Corrected 2026-09-25 by the independent review (F3):** the override fired for any file under `.github/workflows/`, so an edit to another workflow waited for a `plan-gates` run that correctly never starts and then escalated; it now fires only for the selected workflow's own file (`--workflow-path`). Leg `ci-wait-another-workflow-edit-reads-through-the-filter` (+1).
- [x] Mark the D00 T04 §30 stop-on-red wording superseded: its stamped item and checkpoint still read that a red conclusion stops the run (plan review of the D00 T04 §31 candidate, PR8; needs §31 shipped: it records §31's supersession). Done when: §30 carries a dated `Corrected` note beside that wording pointing at D00 T04 §31, with the stamp untouched, quoted. Done: D00 T04 §30 carries a `**Corrected 2026-09-25**` note after its checkpoint that quotes both stop-on-red phrases and points at D00 T04 §31, with its stamp untouched; the `ci-wait` CLI comment that still said `red stops the run` now says a red is repaired. `check-anchors` on §30 still reads `cites resolve`.
- [x] Commit: `"workspace: CI repair-loop follow-ups"` Done: `bbb8869c`, with the review answers in `78f2ae26`, `d70deba9`, and `75dad62c`.

**Test checkpoint:** The end-to-end episode is recorded; the owner rule, the setup-cause rule, and the episode cap read in the skills; the log-unavailable, slow-run, and workflow-change fixture legs pass; §30 carries its supersession note.
