## 34. Campaign Guard Follow-Ups

Started: 2026-09-25T17:28:42Z.

The D00 T04 §32 plan review files seven findings the §32 contract does not own: the guard and an escalation report collide (PR1), run-end cleanup is stated but unproven (PR2), the stall fingerprint misses untracked content (PR4) and counts bookkeeping as progress (PR5), guard ownership is not exclusive (PR6), the heartbeat lifecycle is unexercised (PR7), and a failing hook goes silent (PR9). PR8 joined the episode-cap item in D00 T04 §33, and PR3 was rejected (`0 runnable now` is the plan's one genuine halt, and the runner still owes its `PARKED` record).

- [x] Hand the guard over before an escalation report: an exhausted repair bound or an unverifiable CI (D00 T04 §31) ends in a report, and the Stop hook would block that turn. (plan review of the D00 T04 §32 candidate, PR1; needs §32 shipped: it hardens §32's guard). Done when: the escalation path writes a column-0 `PARKED` line naming the cause (or deletes the guard) before reporting, and a hook fixture proves the report turn ends, quoted. Done: both `review-todo-section` and `process-phase` now end the run before an escalation report: a column-0 `PARKED <UTC> escalation: <cause>` line, then `python scripts/campaign_guard.py end --reason escalation` and `CronDelete` of the heartbeat it names; `process-plan` states the same and the hook's block message names the path. `end --reason escalation` refuses without that `PARKED ... escalation:` line. Legs: `the-escalation-report-turn-ends` (+1, campaign_guard), `end-escalation-leaves-nothing-behind` (counted under item 2), pin `skill-ci-escalation-ends-run` (+1, review_prompt). **Corrected 2026-09-25 by the independent review (F5):** the hook's block message advertised `end --reason escalation` without `--session`, which `end` refuses, so following it left the run open; it now names the owning session and the `CronDelete`, and `end` refuses an empty session by name. Legs `the-escalation-instruction-names-the-session`, `end-refuses-without-a-session` (+2).
- [x] Prove every run-end cleanup: closeout, park, plan done, and operator stop each delete the guard file, the state file, and the heartbeat job. (plan review of the D00 T04 §32 candidate, PR2; needs §32 shipped: it hardens §32's guard). Done when: a driven check per end path shows all three gone afterwards, quoted, and a later session is neither blocked nor resumed by leftovers. Done: `python scripts/campaign_guard.py end --session S --reason closeout|park|plan-done|operator-stop|escalation` checks the path's marker, deletes the guard file and the state file, and names the heartbeat job to `CronDelete`; `process-plan` routes every end path through it. Driven in a fixture workspace against a real one-shot job per path (`7aa4d03f`, `8095d161`, `675831eb`, `03235caf`, `980f4342`): each `end` left `build/` empty, each job `Cancelled`, `CronList` afterwards held only the live heartbeat, and the hook then allowed both a later session and the same one, quoted in docs/phase-runs/2026-09-25-phase-0.md. Legs (+7): `end-<path>-leaves-nothing-behind` for all five paths, `end-refuses-a-closeout-without-its-heading`, `end-refuses-another-session`; pin `skill-ends-through-end` (+1). **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 2:** the heartbeat's own end branches (the plan run over, a twice-stalled run) and the terminal acceptance deleted the guard and state directly, bypassing the lock and the session check; they now run `end --reason plan-done` or the new `end --reason stall` (refused below two trips), and `process-plan` states that every end goes through `end`. Legs `end-refuses-a-stall-without-two-trips`, `end-stall-leaves-nothing-behind`, pin `skill-heartbeat-ends-through-end` (+3).
- [x] Fingerprint untracked content, not just the untracked set: an edit to a new, still-untracked file is real progress the breaker currently cannot see. (plan review of the D00 T04 §32 candidate, PR4; needs §32 shipped: it hardens §32's guard). Done when: the hook hashes untracked file contents (bounded), and a fixture editing an untracked file resets the breaker, quoted. Done: the hook hashes each untracked file with `git hash-object -- <paths>` (bounded: 500 files, 4MB each, in chunks of 50; larger files count by size and write time). Getting it working found a real defect: a path list piped into a native command reached git with a BOM on its first line (the default UTF8 `$OutputEncoding`, and under a pwsh 7 parent even a BOM-free one), so the first path named no file and the hash was silently empty. The paths now ride the argument list, and the leg passes both run directly and under `check-all`. Leg `untracked-content-edit-resets-the-breaker` (+1). **Corrected 2026-09-25 by the independent review (F3, F4):** git's default quoting octal-escaped a non-ASCII name so its content was never hashed, and paths past the 500th dropped out of the fingerprint entirely. The list is now read NUL-delimited (`ls-files -z`), content is hashed for the first 500 files, size and write time cover the next 5000, and every later path still counts by name. Legs `a-non-ascii-untracked-edit-is-progress` (an edit only the content hash can see) and `a-file-past-the-hash-bound-is-progress` (+2); each fails its own mutation by name.
- [x] Exclude bookkeeping from the stall fingerprint: a run file that only gains another heartbeat or retry line reads as progress and keeps the breaker from tripping. (plan review of the D00 T04 §32 candidate, PR5; needs §32 shipped: it hardens §32's guard). Done when: the fingerprint ignores the run file's Critical-events lines (or an equivalent bookkeeping marker), and a fixture that appends only a heartbeat line still trips the breaker, quoted. Done: the run file leaves the git diff and the untracked set and is hashed on its own with its `## Critical events` section stripped, so a heartbeat or retry line there is not progress while a Sections entry is. Legs (+2): `a-bookkeeping-line-alone-still-trips-the-breaker`, `a-sections-entry-is-progress`. The live drive's pre-idle Critical-events note did not reset the breaker.
- [x] Make guard ownership exclusive: two sessions could overwrite one guard file or schedule duplicate heartbeats. (plan review of the D00 T04 §32 candidate, PR6; needs §32 shipped: it hardens §32's guard). Done when: the guard is acquired atomically (create-exclusive or compare-and-swap on `session_id`), a second session refuses to take a live guard without an operator handover, and fixtures pin both, quoted. Done: `python scripts/campaign_guard.py acquire` creates the guard with `O_EXCL`, lets its owner re-point it by compare-and-swap on `session_id`, refuses a live guard to another session, and takes one over only with the operator's `--handover <reason>`, recorded in the guard as `handover_from` and `handover_reason`; `process-plan` acquires and re-points through it. Legs (+5): `acquire-creates-the-guard`, `acquire-refuses-a-second-session`, `acquire-re-points-its-own-guard`, `acquire-hands-over-with-a-reason`, `acquire-refuses-an-escaping-run-file`; pin `skill-acquires-the-guard` (+1). Live: this run's guard was re-pointed to `c0da3c54`, and a second session's `acquire` exited 1. **Corrected 2026-09-25 by the independent review (F1, F2):** the owner's re-point re-read the guard and then replaced it, so a handover landing in between was overwritten; every guard change (acquire, re-point, handover, end) now holds an interprocess lock (**Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 1:** an operating-system lock on `build/claude-campaign-guard.lock`, `msvcrt.locking` on Windows and `flock` elsewhere, which never expires while its holder lives and is released by the OS when the holder dies, replacing a create-exclusive file broken by age, which could break a live holder's lock; the plan query `end --reason plan-done` needs now runs before the lock). A refused `acquire` left its freshly created heartbeat alive; `process-plan` now cancels that job and starts nothing, and the canonical heartbeat prompt replies `NOT THE OWNER` and deletes itself when the guard names another session, as its first step, before `hook-error --session` reads or clears anything (**Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 1**). Legs `a-held-lock-refuses-a-change` (a live holder in another process), `the-lock-is-released-after-a-change`, `a-dead-holder-releases-the-lock`, `concurrent-changes-serialize`, `hook-error-refuses-another-sessions-state`, pins `skill-refused-acquire-cancels-its-job`, `skill-heartbeat-checks-ownership`, `skill-heartbeat-owner-first` (+8).
- [x] Drive the heartbeat lifecycle: the recovery path (CronCreate, idle fire, resume of the named session, 7-day replacement, delete on stop) is specified but unexercised. (plan review of the D00 T04 §32 candidate, PR7; needs §32 shipped: it hardens §32's guard). Done when: a recorded drive creates the job, sees it fire into an idle session and resume, replaces it, and deletes it on stop, each step quoted. Done: recorded in docs/phase-runs/2026-09-25-phase-0.md. Created (`d9ac17ce` at run start), replaced (`d9ac17ce` cancelled, `c0da3c54` created on the new canonical prompt, guard re-pointed), fired into the idle session after the breaker let the turn end (blocks 1 to 3, trip at `17:37:41Z`), resumed the campaign at `17:40:00Z` at the open section, and deleted on stop (the end-path drive cancelled a real job on every path, operator stop included).
- [x] Surface a failed hook: the hook fails open on a thrown error, so a broken guard silently stops guarding. (plan review of the D00 T04 §32 candidate, PR9; needs §32 shipped: it hardens §32's guard). Done when: a failure writes a line to `build/claude-campaign-state.json` (or a hook log), the next heartbeat reports it in the run file, and a fixture that breaks the hook proves the report, quoted. Done: the hook still fails open, but a thrown error now writes `hook_error` and `hook_error_at` to `build/claude-campaign-state.json`; `python scripts/campaign_guard.py hook-error` prints and clears it, and the canonical heartbeat prompt runs it first and appends any line to the run file's Critical events. Legs (+2): `a-thrown-error-is-recorded`, `hook-error-reports-then-clears`; pin `skill-heartbeat-reports-hook-errors` (+1). The live heartbeat's step 1 printed nothing. **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 2:** `hook-error` now holds the guard lock through its ownership check and clear, and reports (rather than refuses) when the guard itself is unreadable, which is the failure it exists to surface. Leg `hook-error-reports-through-a-malformed-guard` (+1), run before the malformed guard is repaired.
- [x] Commit: `"workspace: campaign guard follow-ups"` Done: `5b1d5eaf`, with the review answers in `c51a519e`, `070bf0f3`, and `94c8b920`.

**Test checkpoint:** The escalation report turn ends, each run-end path leaves nothing behind, the untracked-edit and bookkeeping-only fixtures behave, a second session is refused a live guard, the heartbeat lifecycle drive is recorded, and a broken hook is reported, all quoted.

## 35. CI Read-Back Follow-Ups

Started: 2026-09-25T18:43:43Z.

The D00 T04 §33 review files what its contract does not own. Its sign-off round (panel round 3) found that `ci-wait`'s no-log fallback does not decode YAML scalars: `workflow_step_commands` keeps a quoted inline `run:` command's enclosing quotes and keeps a folded `>` block's newlines, so the command it prints for a local re-run can differ from the one GitHub executes. The same function was patched in panel rounds 1, 2, and 3 running (indentation, a dash-led block's sibling keys, then this), so the review skill's three-round rule stops the patching and this section rethinks it. The D00 T04 §33 plan review (run 20260925-D00-T04-S33-astra) adds eleven accepted findings: §31's superseded rules still read (PR1), a run pending past the ceiling has no outcome of its own (PR2), a zero-job red and a mixed-cause log are judged without stated evidence rules (PR3, PR4), a decoded command still lacks its run context, authoritative expectations, and a stated YAML subset (PR5, PR6, PR7), the episode count does not survive a restart (PR9), the drill left bound exhaustion and a stamped-owner repair unexercised (PR10), an authorized workflow retirement always escalates (PR11), and unsupported command forms must fail closed until this section ships (PR12).

- [x] Decode `run:` scalars the way GitHub does, or state the forms read: `workflow_step_commands` in `scripts/review_prompt.py` returns raw scalar text, so `run: "python3 scripts/check.py"` prints with its quotes and `run: >` prints unfolded (panel round 3 of the D00 T04 §33 review, F8; needs §33 shipped: it rethinks §33's command fallback). Done when: plain, single-quoted, double-quoted (with escapes), literal `|`, and folded `>` scalars, each with `-`/`+` chomping, decode to the command text GitHub runs, one fixture leg per form quoted; any form still unsupported prints `ci-wait: rerun locally: <step>: unsupported run: form, read the workflow` instead of a guessed command, quoted from a leg; and the three earlier block legs re-pass. Cheaper substitute that fails the checkpoint: a fourth ad hoc patch for the two forms the round named. Done: `workflow_steps` in `scripts/review_prompt.py` decodes plain (comment stripped), single-quoted (`''`), double-quoted (every YAML escape, `\x`, `\u`, `\U`), literal `|` and folded `>` blocks with `-`/`+` chomping and an indentation indicator, per YAML 1.2; anchors, aliases, tags, flow collections, multi-line quoted or plain scalars, and unknown escapes refuse as `ci-wait: rerun locally at <sha>: <step>: unsupported run: form (<why>), read the workflow`. The expected outputs come from GitHub: a disposable `scalar-echo.yml` ran every form under `shell: cat {0}` (run 36175449390), and its eleven step outputs are `SCALAR_ECHO_GITHUB`. Legs (+23): `scalar-decodes-as-github: <form>` for all eleven, `scalar-set-covers-every-github-step`, `scalar-chomping-clip-strip-keep`, and five `scalar-refuses` plus five `rerun-refuses-unsupported` (anchor, multi-line double, multi-line plain, unknown escape, flow sequence); dropping the double-quote decoding fails `scalar-decodes-as-github: double quoted` by name. The earlier block legs re-pass.
- [x] Mark §31's per-red bound and step-name escalation superseded: D00 T04 §31's stamped items still state `at most three repair attempts per red` and an escalation for a red whose failing step never ran the repository's code, which D00 T04 §33 replaced with the per-episode cap and the rule by cause (plan review of the D00 T04 §33 candidate, PR1; needs §33 shipped). Done when: §31 carries a dated `Corrected` note beside each superseded rule pointing at D00 T04 §33, its stamp untouched, quoted. Done: D00 T04 §31 carries a `**Corrected 2026-09-25**` note after its checkpoint quoting both superseded rules and pointing at D00 T04 §33, its stamp untouched; `check-anchors` on §31 reads `cites resolve`.
- [x] Give a run pending past its ceiling an outcome of its own: `ci-wait` reports a listed run still queued or in progress at `--ceiling` with the same `not concluded` line as an unreachable GitHub (plan review of the D00 T04 §33 candidate, PR2; needs §33 shipped). Done when: that case prints `still <status> past the <N>s ceiling` with its own exit code, distinct from unverifiable, a fixture leg pins it, and both skills say whether the run waits again, escalates, or parks on it, quoted. Done: `ci_conclusion` returns exit 3 with `ci-wait: <sha> <workflow> still <status> past the <N>s ceiling (run <id>): re-run ci-wait once, then escalate a queue that never drains`, distinct from unverifiable (2), and the CLI does not auto-retry it; both skills state the rule. The pending leg now asserts exit 3; pin `skill-ci-ceiling-exit` (+1).
- [x] State the evidence rules for a red's cause: a zero-job red reads repairable from `"jobs": []` alone, and a log carrying both a repository error and a platform signal reads platform because the platform check runs first (plan review of the D00 T04 §33 candidate, PR3 and PR4; needs §33 shipped). Done when: a zero-job red reads repairable only with the run's `startup_failure` conclusion or a workflow-file annotation, else `cause: unknown`; mixed-cause logs follow a stated precedence (the signal that ended the job decides); fixtures cover missing job metadata, an ambiguous zero-job run, and both mixed orders, quoted. Done: a zero-job red reads repairable only on GitHub's own evidence (a `startup_failure` conclusion, or `gh run view` saying the run likely failed because of a workflow file issue, which the real drill red `784d4fe6` shows, quoted live), else `cause: unknown`; mixed logs are decided by the signal that ended the job (the last platform line against the last repository error, cancellation and exit-code lines counting as neither). Legs (+3): `ci-wait-zero-jobs-without-evidence-reads-unknown`, `ci-wait-mixed-cause-the-ending-signal-decides: mixedrepo` and `: mixedplatform`; the earlier zero-job, shutdown-then-cancel, and missing-metadata legs re-pass.
- [x] Print a re-run command with its context or refuse it: a decoded `run:` line still depends on the step's `shell`, `working-directory`, `env`, `${{ }}` expressions, matrix values, and earlier steps (plan review of the D00 T04 §33 candidate, PR5, PR6, and PR7; needs §33 shipped: it extends the command fallback beside the scalar item above). Done when: the supported YAML subset is stated (scalar forms, chomping, indentation indicators) with every other form refused; a step whose context the fallback cannot reproduce prints `cannot reproduce locally: <reason>` rather than a command; and the decoder's expected outputs come from a disposable workflow that echoes each decoded fixture on GitHub, not from self-authored strings, quoted. Done: the supported subset is stated in `workflow_steps`' docstring; `rerun_lines` prints a one-line command with its `cd <working-directory> &&` and `KEY=value` env prefix and a `(shell: ...; earlier steps assumed run)` line, or a multi-line script as written under the same context line, and refuses `cannot reproduce locally: <reason>` for `${{ }}` expressions (matrix, secrets, contexts) in the command, env, or directory, and for workflow or job `env` or `defaults`. The decoder's expectations are GitHub's (item 1). Legs (+3): `rerun-prints-its-context`, `rerun-refuses-expressions`, `rerun-refuses-workflow-env`. **Corrected 2026-09-25 by the independent review (F2, F3, F4):** env forms the parser does not read (a flow mapping, a block or empty value, workflow-level flow `env`) were dropped or misread, context values were interpolated unquoted and the env reached only the first command, and the default shell was assumed to be Bash. The context now renders as a quoted script (`export KEY='value'` lines and `cd 'dir'` ahead of the command, so the env reaches the whole script), unreadable env forms refuse, and the shell comes from the job's `runs-on` (`bash -e {0}` on Linux and macOS, `pwsh` on Windows, refused when the runner is unknown or a non-POSIX shell would need env rendering). Legs (+7): `rerun-quotes-values-and-scopes-env-to-the-script`, five `rerun-refuses-unreadable-context` (flow env, block env, flow workflow env, unknown runner, windows env), `rerun-names-the-windows-default-shell`; the context leg now expects the quoted script. **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 1:** a block-valued `shell` or `working-directory` was read as its header (`cd '>-'`); both refuse now. Legs `: block working-directory`, `: block shell` (+2). **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 2:** a job running in a `container:` (or with `services:`) was advertised with the host's default shell; it refuses now, the container's shell and filesystem not being the host's. Leg `: container job` (+1).
- [x] Persist the repair episode across a restart: the three-attempt cap lives in run-file prose, so a resumed runner can recount from zero (plan review of the D00 T04 §33 candidate, PR9; needs §33 shipped). Done when: an episode id (first red's sha) and its attempt count persist beside the run guard state, a resumed runner reads them before its next repair push, and a fixture restarts mid-episode and still refuses a fourth attempt, quoted. Done: `python scripts/campaign_guard.py repair attempt --red <sha> --commit <sha>` persists the episode (id = the first red's sha) in `build/claude-campaign-repair.json` under the guard lock and refuses a fourth attempt with exit 1; `repair close --green <sha>` ends it and `repair status` reads it for a resumed runner; both skills route every repair push through it. Legs (+4): `repair-attempts-count-across-processes`, `repair-status-reads-the-persisted-episode`, `repair-refuses-a-fourth-attempt-after-a-restart`, `repair-close-opens-a-fresh-episode`; pin `skill-ci-episode-persists` (+1). **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 1:** an unreadable or malformed episode file read as no episode, which reset the bound; it now refuses (exit 1, escalate). Legs `repair-refuses-corrupt-state-rather-than-resetting`, `repair-refuses-malformed-state-rather-than-resetting` (+2). **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 2:** a file holding JSON `null` still read as no episode; only a missing file does now. Leg `repair-refuses-null-state-rather-than-resetting` (+1).
- [x] Drill the rest of the repair loop: the D00 T04 §33 drill closed green at 2 of 3 and needed no stamped owner (plan review of the D00 T04 §33 candidate, PR10; needs §33 shipped). Done when: a disposable drill exhausts the bound (three failed repairs, then the escalation report naming the cause) and a drill repairs a red owned by a stamped fixture section (reopen, repaired candidate, re-review, re-stamp), each episode quoted in a run file, the disposable branches deleted. Done: recorded in docs/phase-runs/2026-09-25-phase-0.md on the disposable `drill/d00-t04-s35-loop` with fixture guard state. Bound exhaustion: three repairs of red `9831ac71` stayed red, the fourth `repair attempt` exited 1 (`the bound is exhausted, escalate`), and the escalation parked, ended the fixture guard, and cancelled its job. Stamped-owner repair: fixture section TODO-90 §1 stamped with a breaking change (`765b8d38` red), reopened, repaired in `b06d9306` (green), re-reviewed by a real `bulk` panel round (round 2 approved all four lenses; round 1 fenced the wrong range and was superseded), and re-stamped in `13def09d` (green). Both branches deleted.
- [x] Verify an authorized workflow retirement or trigger change: a range that removes or re-triggers the selected workflow waits for a run that will never come and escalates (plan review of the D00 T04 §33 candidate, PR11; needs §33 shipped). Done when: `ci-wait` accepts an operator-authorized `--expect-no-run <reason>` for such a range, prints it, exits 0 only when no run appears within the timeout, refuses it on a range that does not touch the workflow, and fixture legs pin all three, quoted. Done: `ci-wait --expect-no-run <reason>` passes (exit 0) only when no run of the workflow appears within the timeout, fails (exit 1) naming a run that appeared, and refuses (exit 2) a range that does not edit the selected workflow. Legs (+3): `ci-wait-expect-no-run-passes-on-silence`, `ci-wait-expect-no-run-fails-when-a-run-appears`, `ci-wait-expect-no-run-refuses-a-range-that-keeps-the-workflow`. **Corrected 2026-09-25 by the independent review (F1):** a failed or malformed `gh` poll read as silence, so `--expect-no-run` could pass without proof; only polls that returned a JSON list count now, and anything else exits 2 as unverifiable. Legs `ci-wait-expect-no-run-refuses-an-unverifiable-poll: gherror` and `: notjson` (+2); forcing a failed poll to read as silence fails the first by name. **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 1:** empty output and a list holding a non-run entry (`[null]`) still read as silence; both are unverifiable now. Legs `: emptylist`, `: nullrun` (+2).

Done when: until the scalar item above lands, a quoted or folded `run:` prints `unsupported run: form, read the workflow` instead of its raw text, pinned by a leg, and the §33 limitation note points here, quoted. ## 36. Campaign Guard Lifecycle Follow-Ups

Started: 2026-09-25T20:00:34Z.

The D00 T04 §34 review files what its contract does not own. Its sign-off round (panel round 3, F11) found the state file still deleted outside the guard lock, at `acquire` time and in the heartbeat's missing-guard branch; the guard lifecycle had been patched in panel rounds 1, 2, and 3 running, so the three-round rule files it rather than patching a fourth time. The D00 T04 §34 plan review (run 20260925-D00-T04-S34-astra) adds eleven accepted findings: hook state writes sit outside the lock (PR1, merged with F11), a same-session obsolete heartbeat is indistinguishable from the current one (PR2), a failed `CronDelete` after `end` leaves no record (PR3), startup has no crash recovery (PR4), the 7-day expiry and a new-session migration are undriven (PR5), the malformed-guard path is unproven end to end and `hook-error` clears before the record is durable (PR6, PR7), coverage past the fingerprint bounds is unstated (PR8), the bookkeeping exclusion is coarse (PR9), terminal markers are not bound to the run (PR10), and nothing checks the heartbeat's health after startup (PR11).

- [x] Serialize every guard-state write under the guard lock: the Stop hook writes `build/claude-campaign-state.json` without the lock, `acquire` time resets and the heartbeat's missing-guard branch delete it directly, so a handover or a new acquisition can have its fresh state overwritten or deleted (panel round 3 of the D00 T04 §34 review, F11, and plan review PR1; needs §34 shipped). Done when: the hook takes the same OS lock around its state read and write (bounded wait, failing open and recording on timeout), `acquire` and a `campaign_guard.py reset-state` command replace every direct deletion, each re-checking ownership or absence under the lock, and fixtures interleave a hook write with a handover and with an `end`, quoted. Done: the Stop hook takes the same OS lock as `campaign_guard.py` around its state read and write (`FileStream.Lock` on one byte of `build/claude-campaign-guard.lock`, the same Win32 byte-range lock `msvcrt.locking` takes; bounded by `CAMPAIGN_LOCK_WAIT_MS`, default 5000), and a lock it cannot get fails open into the append-only `build/claude-campaign-hook-errors.log`; `acquire` resets the state under the lock when it creates, hands over, or moves a run, and `python scripts/campaign_guard.py reset-state` (owning `--session`, or `--expect-no-guard yes` for the heartbeat's run-over branch) replaces every direct delete, re-checking ownership or absence under the lock. Legs (+5): `the-hook-waits-for-the-guard-lock` (a Python holder in another process, the hook blocking behind it for over a second), `a-lock-timeout-fails-open-into-the-error-log`, `reset-state-refuses-another-session`, `reset-state-refuses-while-a-guard-lives`, `reset-state-with-no-guard`. **Corrected 2026-09-25 by the independent review (F2):** an error the hook could only write to the fallback log never reached the heartbeat; `hook-error` now reads that log's oldest line when the state holds none, and its exact `--ack` drains it. Legs `hook-error-reads-the-fallback-log`, `hook-error-ack-drains-the-fallback-log` (+2). **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 1:** locking the write alone let a hook that read the old guard, then waited, overwrite a handed-over run's state or recreate an ended run's; the hook now re-reads the guard under the lock and writes nothing when the owner, run file, or run id changed. The fallback log became one file per error under `build/claude-campaign-hook-errors/`, so a drain never races a writer, and the phase transition's direct state delete is gone (`acquire` resets it under the lock). Legs `a-handover-while-the-hook-waits-writes-no-state` and `an-end-while-the-hook-waits-writes-no-state` (real interleavings: another process holds the lock, the hook reads the guard and waits, the change lands under the held lock) (+2); the error-log leg now reads the file. **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 2:** the hook's error path wrote state under the lock without the re-check; it re-checks now and, when the guard changed, writes the error to a file of its own. The interleaving fixture feeds the hook its payload from a file, so `communicate()` never touches a closed pipe (the round named a `ValueError`; three consecutive runs had passed, but the fragility is gone).
- [x] Bind the heartbeat to an acquisition generation: session identity alone cannot tell a replaced heartbeat from the current one in the same session (plan review of the D00 T04 §34 candidate, PR2; needs §34 shipped). Done when: `acquire` writes a generation id beside `cron_id`, the canonical prompt carries it, a heartbeat whose generation or job id is not the guard's replies `NOT THE CURRENT JOB` and deletes itself, and a fixture pins it, quoted. Done: `acquire` records a `generation` (minted by `campaign_guard.py mint-generation`) beside `cron_id`, the canonical prompt carries it, and the heartbeat's first step runs `whoami --session --generation`, which prints `OWNER`, `NOT THE OWNER`, `NOT THE CURRENT JOB`, `NO GUARD`, or `MALFORMED GUARD`; a job that is not the run's deletes itself. Legs (+5): `whoami-owner`, `whoami-not-the-current-job`, `whoami-not-the-owner`, `a-replaced-job-is-not-current`, `whoami-no-guard`; pin `skill-heartbeat-owner-first` now names `whoami`.
- [x] Keep a pending cancellation until `CronDelete` is confirmed: `end` deletes the guard before the job is cancelled, so a failed `CronDelete` loses the job id (plan review PR3; needs §34 shipped). Done when: `end` leaves a `build/claude-campaign-pending-cancel.json` naming the job, the runner clears it only after `CronList` confirms the job gone, the heartbeat retries a pending cancel, and a failed-delete fixture recovers, quoted. Done: `end` writes `build/claude-campaign-pending-cancel.json` naming the job before deleting the guard; `pending-cancel` prints it and `cancel-confirmed --cron-id` clears it only for that job, after `CronList` confirms it gone; the heartbeat retries a pending cancel as its step 3. Legs (+3): `end-leaves-a-pending-cancellation`, `cancel-confirmed-refuses-another-job`, `cancel-confirmed-clears-the-pending-record`; pin `skill-confirms-the-cancel` (+1). **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 1:** a later `end` overwrote an earlier unconfirmed cancellation; the record is now a list, `cancel-confirmed` drains one job at a time, and `reconcile` names every unconfirmed one. Legs `pending-cancellations-accumulate`, `reconcile-surfaces-a-pending-cancellation`, `pending-cancellations-drain-one-by-one` (+3). **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 2:** the heartbeat's run-over and stall branches cleared the pending record straight after `CronDelete`; they confirm with `CronList` first now. Pin `skill-heartbeat-confirms-before-clearing` (+1).
- [x] Recover an interrupted startup: a crash between `CronCreate`, `acquire`, and the run-file record leaves an orphan job or a guard without a heartbeat (plan review PR4; needs §34 shipped). Done when: `process-plan` startup reconciles a job without a guard and a guard without a job before starting, and a fixture for each half recovers, quoted. Done: `process-plan` startup begins with `campaign_guard.py reconcile --session --jobs <live heartbeat ids>`, which names an orphan or stale job to delete, a guard whose job is not live to recreate, and a guard another session owns (start nothing). Legs (+3): `reconcile-consistent`, `reconcile-guard-without-job-and-stale-job`, `reconcile-orphan-job`; pin `skill-reconciles-before-starting` (+1).
- [x] Drive the expiry and new-session paths: the §34 drive proved create, idle fire, replacement, and delete, not the 7-day expiry replacement or a resume in a new session (plan review PR5; needs §34 shipped). Done when: a recorded drive replaces a job at its expiry boundary (or a shortened test expiry, stated) and resumes a run in a new session through `acquire --handover`, the old session's heartbeat replying `NOT THE OWNER`, quoted. Done: `acquire` records `job_created_at` and `campaign_guard.py expiry` reports `replace due` inside 12 hours of the 7-day limit. Drive recorded in docs/phase-runs/2026-09-25-phase-0.md against real jobs `f4e2fc36` and `b250428b`, the boundary reached through `expiry --now` 6 days 13 hours on (stated, not waited): `exit 1: expiry: replace due ... expires in 11.0h`, the replacement re-pointed, the old job's `whoami` reading `NOT THE CURRENT JOB`; then an `acquire --handover` by a second session, the first session's heartbeat reading `NOT THE OWNER`. The live run's own check found its guard predating the field (`the job's creation time is unknown: replace the job`) and replaced `a81f5330` with `44109a99`. Legs (+2): `expiry-ok-when-fresh`, `expiry-replace-due-near-seven-days`.
- [x] Prove the malformed-guard report end to end and make the clear durable: the heartbeat checks ownership first but a malformed guard names no owner, and `hook-error` clears the record before the run file holds it (plan review PR6 and PR7; needs §34 shipped). Done when: the canonical prompt states the malformed-guard branch, a fixture carries a hook error through a malformed guard into the run file's Critical events, and `hook-error` only clears after a `--ack` naming the recorded line, so an interrupted heartbeat loses nothing, quoted. Done: the canonical prompt's step 1 routes `MALFORMED GUARD` through step 2 and a report; `hook-error` now prints without clearing and clears only with `--ack` naming the exact printed line. The drive carried a real hook error through a malformed guard into a fixture run file's Critical events, then acknowledged it (quoted in the run file). Legs (+2): `hook-error-refuses-a-mismatched-ack`, `hook-error-clears-on-the-exact-ack`; the earlier clear legs now acknowledge.
- [x] State the fingerprint's degraded coverage: past 5500 untracked files an edit to an existing path cannot reset the breaker, and a large file's metadata can move without content (plan review PR8; needs §34 shipped). Done when: the hook and `process-plan` state the policy (what counts past each bound), and boundary fixtures at 500, 5500, and the size limit pin it, quoted. Done: the hook's header and `process-plan` state the policy (content hashed for the first 500 untracked files, size and write time for the next 5000, the name past that); the bounds read `CAMPAIGN_HASH_FILES`, `CAMPAIGN_STAT_FILES`, and `CAMPAIGN_HASH_BYTES`, so the fixtures pin each boundary at small bounds. Legs (+4): `coverage-a-hashed-file-edit-counts-even-with-its-time-kept`, `coverage-past-the-hash-bound-a-same-size-same-time-edit-is-invisible`, `coverage-past-the-hash-bound-a-time-change-counts`, `coverage-past-the-stat-bound-only-the-name-counts`. **Corrected 2026-09-25 by panel round 1:** no leg exercised `CAMPAIGN_HASH_BYTES`; leg `coverage-a-file-past-the-size-bound-counts-by-size-and-time` (+1) pins it.
- [x] Mark bookkeeping explicitly: stripping the whole Critical events section can hide real recovery work logged there (plan review PR9; needs §34 shipped). Done when: bookkeeping lines carry an explicit marker (for example a leading `bookkeeping:`), only those are excluded, a substantive Critical events line resets the breaker, and fixtures pin both, quoted. Done: only `- bookkeeping: ...` lines leave the fingerprint, anywhere in the run file; the canonical prompt writes its resume note that way. Legs: `a-substantive-critical-events-line-is-progress` (+1); the bookkeeping leg now uses the marker; pin `skill-marks-bookkeeping` (+1).
- [x] Bind terminal markers to the run: a reused run file's old `PARKED` or `## Closeout` could release a new campaign (plan review PR10; needs §34 shipped: it extends §32's marker semantics). Done when: the guard records the run file's length (or a run id) at acquisition, the hook and `end` honour only markers written after it, and a reused-run-file fixture keeps blocking, quoted. Done: `acquire` records `run_offset`, the run file's byte length at acquisition (kept on a same-run re-point); the hook and `end` honour `## Closeout` and `PARKED` only after it, and a file shorter than the offset counts none. Legs (+2): `a-reused-run-files-old-marker-keeps-blocking`, `a-marker-after-acquisition-ends-the-run`. **Corrected 2026-09-25 by the independent review (F1):** a byte offset breaks when the run file is edited above it (process-phase puts the park record under Gap audit, so a new `PARKED` could sit before the offset, and text inserted above an old marker could push it past); markers now carry the guard's `run_id` (`## Closeout run=<id>`, `PARKED <UTC> run=<id> <reason>`), wherever they sit, `whoami` prints it (`OWNER run=<id>`), and the skills write it; a guard predating run ids accepts any marker. Legs `a-mid-file-marker-carrying-the-run-id-ends-the-run`, `an-old-marker-pushed-down-the-file-keeps-blocking`, `whoami-owner-names-the-run`, pin `skill-markers-carry-the-run-id` (+3, one renamed).
- [x] Check the heartbeat's health during the run: nothing notices a missing, expired, or mismatched job after startup (plan review PR11; needs §34 shipped). Done when: each section boundary runs `CronList` against the guard's `cron_id`, recreates a missing job through `acquire`, and the run file records the repair, with a fixture or drive quoted. Done: `campaign_guard.py health --session --jobs` names a missing job, and `process-plan` and `process-phase` run it with `expiry` after each stamp, replacing a missing or expiring job before the next section. Live: `health: ok, job 44109a99 is live` and `expiry: ok, job 44109a99 expires in 168.0h` after the migration. Legs (+2): `health-ok`, `health-names-a-missing-job`; pin `skill-checks-health-at-each-boundary` (+1).
- [ ] Commit: `"workspace: campaign guard lifecycle follow-ups"`

**Test checkpoint:** Unit test: `python scripts/campaign_guard.py --self-test` carries the interleaving, generation, pending-cancel, startup-recovery, malformed-report, coverage-boundary, bookkeeping-marker, and marker-binding legs, quoted with the suite count. Driven run with evidence: the expiry-replacement and new-session drive and a health-check repair, each in a run file, quoted.

## 37. CI Read-Back Hardening

The D00 T04 §35 review files what its contract does not own. Its sign-off round (panel round 3, F10 and F11) found the re-run context parser still order-dependent (a job's `container:` or `runs-on:` declared after its steps is missed) and blind to quoted step keys (`"shell": pwsh`); the context parser was patched in the independent pass and panel rounds 1, 2, and 3 running, so the three-round rule files both here rather than patching a fourth time. The D00 T04 §35 plan review (run 20260925-D00-T04-S35-astra) adds thirteen accepted findings, grouped below by the unit they harden.

- [ ] Parse the owning job whole, and quoted keys, or refuse: `workflow_steps` reads job context by scanning backwards from the step and matches only bare keys, so key order and quoting change the printed command (panel round 3 of the D00 T04 §35 review, F10 and F11; needs §35 shipped). Done when: each step's job context (`runs-on`, `container`, `services`, `defaults`, `env`) is read from the whole owning job regardless of key order, quoted and plain keys decode alike or the step refuses as unsupported, and fixtures cover a job declaring `container:` and `runs-on:` after its steps plus each quoted context key, quoted.
- [ ] Tie a red's cause to the failed step and give `cause: unknown` a next action: the ending-signal rule reads line order alone, and an unknown cause has no decision (plan review of the D00 T04 §35 candidate, PR1 and PR2; needs §35 shipped). Done when: platform and repository signals are correlated with the failed job and step (an ambiguous correlation reads `cause: unknown`), the skills state the bounded evidence gathering an unknown cause owes (the run page, the full log, a local re-run) and its escalation, and fixtures pin a cross-step signal and the unknown path, quoted.
- [ ] Separate reproducible commands from diagnostic scripts, cover `uses:` failures, and prove execution context: `earlier steps assumed run` hides checkout, generated files, installed tools, and inherited environment; a failing `uses:` step lists unrelated `run:` commands; and the scalar oracle proved text, not context (plan review PR3, PR4, and PR5; needs §35 shipped). Done when: the fallback labels a command reproducible only when its prerequisites are named and otherwise prints it as a diagnostic script; a failing `uses:` step names its action and reports no local reproduction; and a disposable workflow echoes each supported shell, env, and working-directory combination the way the scalar drill did, its outputs becoming the fixture's expectations, quoted.
- [ ] Bind the repair episode and make its lifecycle idempotent: the episode carries no repository, branch, workflow, or campaign identity, an attempt is counted before or after its push by convention alone, `repair close` accepts any sha, and a lost `build/` file silently starts over (plan review PR6, PR7, PR8, and PR9; needs §35 shipped). Done when: the episode records its repository, branch, workflow, and run file and refuses a mismatched call; an attempt is keyed to its repair commit so a repeat after a crash or failed push counts once; `repair close` requires a green read-back of that workflow on a descendant of the episode's last attempt; a lost episode file during an open run is detected from the run file's attempt lines and restored or escalated; fixtures pin each, quoted.
- [ ] Account for the ceiling re-run: `re-run ci-wait once` has no persisted count and no disposition for a run still in progress after it (plan review PR10; needs §35 shipped). Done when: the one allowed re-run is recorded beside the repair episode, a second exit 3 on the same run escalates, a restart does not reset the count, and fixtures pin both queued and in-progress runs, quoted.
- [ ] Record the no-run authorization and keep its outcome distinct: `--expect-no-run` needs only a workflow edit and a reason, and its exit 0 reads like green (plan review PR11 and PR12; needs §35 shipped). Done when: the flag requires an authorization record (who, when, the approved range) and a trigger change in the range, prints and exits with an outcome distinct from green, `repair close` refuses it as evidence, and fixtures pin each, quoted.
- [ ] Redact what the fallback copies into records: workflow commands, env values, and log excerpts land in review and phase records verbatim (plan review PR13; needs §35 shipped). Done when: `ci-wait` masks values that look like secrets (GitHub's `***` masks kept, token and key shapes, env values named like secrets), the skills say records quote only the redacted form, and redaction fixtures pass, quoted.
- [ ] Commit: `"workspace: CI read-back hardening"`

**Test checkpoint:** Unit test: `python scripts/review_prompt.py --self-test` and `python scripts/campaign_guard.py --self-test` carry the job-context, quoted-key, correlation, unknown-cause, `uses:`, episode-binding, idempotent-attempt, close-evidence, lost-state, ceiling-count, authorization, and redaction legs, quoted with the suite counts. Driven run with evidence: the execution-context echo workflow's run, quoted in a run file.
